L288: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L289: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L290: All pivotal nonclinical studies were conducted in good laboratory practice (GLP)-compliant testing facilities in the US, that are signatories of the Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development Mutual Acceptance of Data program in accordance with the Organization for Economic Cooperation and Development Test Guidelines and Principles of GLP.
L291: While the target (DLL3) expression is low and mainly cytoplasmic in normal cells, DLL3 is
L292: 63
L293: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L294: **Disclaimer:** In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
L295: upregulated in neuroendocrine tumor cells and localizes to the tumor cell surface. This differential expression profile makes DLL3 a compelling, tumor-selective therapeutic target. Therefore, tarlatamab-associated changes observed in the toxicology studies were limited and are consistent with the expected mechanism of action given that DLL3 has a limited tissue distribution combined with low levels of intracellular expression.
L296: The cynomolgus monkey was selected as species for nonclinical safety evaluation of tarlatamab based on target binding affinity and bioactivity data. The IV route of administration was chosen for in vivo nonclinical studies based on the intended clinical route of administration. Due to the physicochemical properties of tarlatamab, the maximal feasible dose that could be administered to cynomolgus monkey was limited to 4500 µg/kg.
L297: The nonclinical safety assessment of tarlatamab was based on a 10-day exploratory as well as 28-day GLP and 3-month GLP repeat-dose IV toxicology studies with once weekly doses of up to 4500 µg/kg.
L298: In the 10-day exploratory toxicology study with dosages of ≤ 1000 µg/kg, tarlatamab-related effects were limited to a body temperature increase in a single high-dose animal on day 10, decreases in lymphocyte populations, minimal increases in C-reactive protein (CRP), monocyte chemoattractant protein-1 (MCP-1) and IFN\gamma serum concentrations. Tarlatamab-related light microscopy findings were limited to minimal and mild mixed inflammatory cell infiltration in lung, liver, and pituitary gland with no indication of cellular damage in any tissue.
L299: In the 28-day study with dosages of ≤ 4500 µg/kg, tarlatamab-related changes consistent with redirected cell lysis were limited to a minor decrease in lymphocyte populations (detected after the second dose only) at 4500 ≤ µg/kg. The only other tarlatamab-related effects were a slightly increased heart rate (on day 2 but not at day 23) at ≥ 500 µg/kg as well as a minimal to mild mixed cell infiltrate comprised of lymphocytes and eosinophils in the pituitary at 50 µg/kg and 500 µg/kg which showed evidence of reversibility by the end of the 28-day recovery period. A dose-related increase in incidence of antidrug antibodies (ADAs) occurred, which potentially contributed to variability in exposure levels at the end of the study. One animal had vascular injury-associated changes in the heart and lung that were consistent with secondary effects likely mediated by ADA-related immune complexes.
L300: In the 3-month study, tarlatamab-related clinical pathology changes were limited to a minimal decrease in lymphocytes at 4500 µg/kg, the maximum dose administered. In addition, tarlatamab-related changes were observed in the pituitary gland at ≥ 50 µg/kg, characterized as a minimal to mild mononuclear cell infiltrate composed primarily of lymphocytes with no evidence of tissue injury.
L301: Local tolerance of IV administration of tarlatamab was evaluated in the 28-day and the 3-month toxicology studies in the cynomolgus monkey. In the 28-day as well as the 3-month study, no evidence of irritation or other local tarlatamab-related effects were observed at the IV administration sites.
L302: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L303: Tarlatamab-related changes are reversible and clinically monitorable, and the highest non-severely toxic dose was determined to be 4500 µg/kg based on the absence of any tarlatamab-related adverse findings.
L304: Following the proposal of the FDA, a murine surrogate molecule of tarlatamab, designated muS757, was developed to evaluate potential effects of a DLL3-targeting BiTE molecule on embryofetal development in mice.
L305: Tarlatamab: 28-Day Intravenous Infusion Toxicology Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey with a 28-Day Recovery Period / Study Number: 121424 / eCTD location: Module 4.2.3.2
Key Drug-related Adverse Findings
No adverse effects on any toxicology or safety pharmacology parameters evaluated at up to the high dose of 4500 µg/kg weekly for 28 days as well as after the 28-day recovery period.
GLP compliance: Yes
L306: Methods
Dose and frequency of dosing:	0, 50, 500, 4500 µg/kg weekly for 28 days;
0 and 500 µg/kg for 28 days and 28-day recovery phase
Route of administration:	IV infusion (4-min infusion)
Formulation/Vehicle:	(b) (4) glutamic acid, (b) (4) sucrose, (b) (4) polysorbate 80, pH 4.2)
Species/Strain:	Monkey/Cynomolgus monkey
Number/Sex/Group:	3/sex/group; 2/sex/group for recovery
Age:	2.5 to 4.6 years
Satellite groups:	None
Major Effects	There were tarlatamab-related slight increases in heart rate at ≥ 500 µg/kg that were associated with decreased PR, RR, and QT intervals. Throughout the 24 hour collection period, the mean change in heart rate was less than 10% higher than the control group.
Changes were not evident in any animal regardless of ADA status on day 23. Given the low magnitude of the heart rate increase and the absence of an effect at the end of study despite continued dosing, this finding was not considered adverse.
L307: 65
L308: Version date: June 2022 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
L309: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L310: At the end of the dosing phase, minimal to mild levels of tarlatamab-related mixed cell infiltrates were observed in the pituitary gland in 3 of 6 animals at both 50 and 500 mg/kg. The infiltrates were comprised predominantly of lymphocytes together with variable-to-abundant numbers of eosinophils and were restricted to the interface between the pars intermedia and pars nervosa, which is consistent with the expression pattern of DLL3 in the pituitary (Study 123377). There was no evidence of tissue injury associated with the infiltrate.
Despite higher exposure levels at 4500 µg/kg, the absence of this change correlated with the presence of ADAs in all animals at this dose level as well as 3 of 6 animals at 500 mg/kg.
After the recovery phase, the mixed cell infiltrate in the pituitary gland was partially reversed, with full reversibility expected with additional time.

Major Effects	Tarlatamab-related clinical pathology changes were limited to a minimal decrease in lymphocytes at 4500 µg/kg. Additional microscopic changes at ≥ 50 µg/kg included a minimal, mononuclear, and/or mixed inflammatory cell infiltrate in multiple tissues (heart, lung, brain, and gallbladder) composed primarily of small foci of lymphocytes with a perivascular orientation. Although the cell infiltrate is similar in composition, severity, and distribution to a spontaneous infiltrate commonly observed in this species, the increased incidence in multiple tissues of animals administered tarlatamab was attributed to the IV administration of a foreign biotherapeutic and/or the presence of ADAs

L332: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L333: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's summaries of the repeat-dose toxicology studies in monkeys. Though the monkey was chosen for toxicity testing based on the ability of tarlatamab to bind human and monkey CD3 and DLL3 similarly in in vitro assays, toxicity associated with tarlatamab may be underrepresented in the monkey given that immunohistochemistry data described above indicates that DLL3 is expressed intracellularly in normal tissues and cells.
L334: In addition to decreases in lymphocytes that were noted in all toxicology studies in monkeys, increases in large unstained cells (up to 213%) and decreases in neutrophils (up to -52%) were noted at ≥50 µg/kg in the 1-month study and decreased eosinophils (up to -76%) were noted at
L335: 4500 µg/kg in the 3-month study compared to controls. All hematologic changes noted in the 1-month study improved after a drug-free recovery period.
L336: Though interpretation of findings from the 10-day study in monkeys is limited by the study design, including lack of a vehicle-treated control and limited number of animals (n=2 females/group), findings suggestive of a systemic inflammatory response were noted in monkeys administered 1000 µg/kg, including transient increases in CRP (300%) and neutrophils (468%) that occurred 4 to 24 hours after the first dose compared to baseline values, as well as histological signs of minimal to mild mixed cell infiltrates in the liver, lung, and pituitary gland.
L337: Changes in lymphocyte populations in monkeys correlated with decreased CD3, CD4, and CD8 T cell populations following the first or second dose of tarlatamab at ≥100 µg/kg in the 10-day study and at 4500 µg/kg in the 1-month study; decreases in T cell populations improved by the end of the dosing phase in the 1-month monkey study. Decreases in B cells and NK cells also occurred at ≥100 µg/kg in the 10-day monkey study.
L338: Similar findings of decreased leukocytes, lymphocytes, and neutrophils were also noted in mice administered ≥500 µg/kg of a mouse tarlatamab surrogate, muS757, and correlated with decreased lymphoid cellularity in the lymph nodes at 4500 µg/kg.
L339: Though signs of anemia were not observed in the 1-month or 3-month studies in monkeys administered tarlatamab or the 1-month study in mice with a mouse surrogate of tarlatamab, hematologic changes noted in the 10-Day study in monkeys included decreases in red blood cells (up to -21%), hemoglobin (up to -20%), and hematocrit (up to -21%) and increased reticulocytes (up to 447%) in animals administered ≥100 µg/kg compared to pre-dose values. These changes occurred 4 hours after the first dose and persisted through the dosing phase.
L340: A transient increase in AST (up to 450% compared to pre-dose values) occurred at 1000 µg/kg in the 10-day study following the first dose but improved by the end of the dosing phase. The relevance of this finding is unclear as there was no histologic correlate and liver changes were not noted at doses ≤4500 µg/kg in the 1-month or 3-month monkey studies.
L341: Though transient increases in heart rate were observed at doses ≥500 µg/kg in the 1-month toxicology study, this finding was only noted in 2 male monkeys on Day 2 and increased heart rate or other ECG changes were not noted in the 3-month monkey study.
L342: Additional findings noted in the 3-month monkey study at doses ≥50 µg/kg include abnormal feces color and decreased thyroid weight, though the relevance of these findings is unclear as no correlates were identified and they only occurred in females.
L343: There were no notable findings in reproductive organs in the 10-day, 1-month, or 3-month
L344: repeat-dose toxicology studies in monkeys.
L345: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L346: ## The Applicant's Position:
L347: No studies evaluating the genotoxic or mutagenic potential of tarlatamab have been conducted. Tarlatamab is a recombinant BiTE molecule made up entirely of naturally-occurring amino acids and contains no inorganic or synthetic organic linkers or other non-protein portions. Therefore, no genotoxicity studies have been conducted or are planned, consistent with ICH Harmonised Tripartite Guideline S6, "Preclinical Safety Evaluation of Biotechnology-Derived Pharmaceuticals" (ICH S6[R1], 2011).
L348: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L349: The FDA agrees.
L350: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L351: ## The Applicant's Position:
L352: No carcinogenicity studies have been conducted nor are any planned in accordance with ICH S9 (ICH, 2010).
L353: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L354: The FDA agrees.
L355: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L356: Following the proposal of the FDA, a murine surrogate molecule of tarlatamab, designated muS757, was developed to evaluate potential effects of a DLL3-targeting BiTE molecule on embryofetal development in mice. The study objectives were to detect potential adverse effects of muS757 on pregnant Crl:CD1 female mice and development of the embryo and fetus consequent to exposure of the female from implantation to closure of the hard palate (Study Number 122517/ eCTD location: Module 4.2.3.5.2).
L357: Timed-mated female mice (22/group) received the vehicle control, 500 or 4500 mg/kg muS757 via IV bolus injection on gestation days (GD) 6 and 13. There were no muS757-related effects on any maternal parameters, including mean maternal body weights or body weight gains. In
L358: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L359: addition, there were no muS757-related macroscopic findings or effects on any ovarian, uterine, or litter parameters at any dose level. Administration of muS757 did not produce any fetal external, visceral, or skeletal malformations or variations.
L360: muS757: An Embryofetal Development Study by Intravenous (Bolus) Injection in Mice / Study Number 122517/ eCTD location: Module 4.2.3.5.2
Study type: Embryofetal development
Key Drug-related Adverse Findings:
No muS757-related maternal effects were observed at any dose level.
There were no muS757-related effects on embryofetal viability or growth, and no muS757-related malformations or variations were observed during external, visceral, or skeletal examination.

L374: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L375: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's conclusion and acknowledges that muS757 (a tarlatamab surrogate that binds mouse CD3 and DLL3) crossed the placental barrier. Additionally, there were no signs of toxicity to male or female reproductive organs in the general toxicology studies in monkeys administered repeat doses of tarlatamab.
L376: Tarlatamab is a bispecific T cell engager and causes T cell-dependent depletion of DLL3-expressing cells. Risks associated with T cell activation include cytokine release and associated inflammatory effects. The occurrence of inflammatory adverse events in the mother may adversely affect the pregnancy.
L377: Studies using genetically engineered mice indicate DLL3 plays roles in skeletal development during embryogenesis. DLL3 was found to be expressed intracellularly in the presomitic mesoderm of mouse embryos and to contribute to somitogenesis, a process leading to segmentation in animals that ultimately gives rise to skeletal, muscle, and other connective
L378: tissues (Chapman et al., 2011). Additional studies have identified DLL3 mRNA transcripts present in unfertilized oocytes and persisting through gastrulation and early organogenesis (Cormier et al., 2004; Dunwoodie et al., 1997). DLL3 loss-of function mutations or deletion of DLL3 in mice leads to delayed and irregular somite formation/segmentation, which results in a phenotype characterized by a disorganized axial skeleton and disruption of vertebral formation, including hemivertebrae associated with disorganized neural arches and rib bifurcations (Dunwoodie et al., 2002, Kusumi et al., 1998)
L379: Further, genetic mutations in DLL3 in humans have been associated with spondylocostal dysostosis, a congenital condition causing segmentation defects of the vertebrae and abnormalities of the ribs that result in a shortened trunk and/or neck (Chapman et al., 2011). Together, these findings suggest that fetal exposure to tarlatamab may increase the risk of abnormal skeletal development.
L380: Given that only DLL3 expressed on the cell surface is expected to be a target for tarlatamab, the intracellular expression pattern of DLL3 noted during fetal development may explain the lack of findings in the EFD study in mice using a mouse tarlatamab surrogate. Though DLL3 has been detected intracellularly during embryogenesis, it remains unknown whether DLL3 is displayed at the cell surface at other timepoints during embryofetal development.
L381: In humans and mice, transfer of IgG antibodies from mother to fetus across the placenta is mediated by the neonatal Fc receptor (FcRn). Tarlatamab can bind the FcRn and may cross the placenta to the developing fetus similarly as an endogenous IgG antibody. Fetal serum concentrations of muS757 were approximately 30-times higher than maternal serum concentrations, indicating potential for tarlatamab to cross the placenta. Additionally, tarlatamab is based on an IgG antibody and may be present in milk; a breastfed child may be exposed to tarlatamab via lactational transfer.
L382: Based on the mechanism of action of tarlatamab, FDA recommends including a Warning for embryo-fetal toxicity and advising females of reproductive potential to use effective contraception during treatment with tarlatamab and for 2 months after the last dose.
L383: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L384: ## The Applicant's Position:
L385: No other toxicology studies using tarlatamab or muS757 were conducted.

L396: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L397: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L398: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L399: The Applicant submitted current BLA 761344 for the proposed indication of tarlatamab monotherapy for the treatment of adult patients with advanced small cell lung cancer (SCLC) with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy. The proposed dosage of tarlatamab is 1 mg on Cycle 1 day 1, followed by 10 mg on Cycle 1 days 8 and 15, and every 2 weeks (Q2W) onward (hereafter referred to as 1/10 mg) administered via a 1-hour intravenous (IV) infusion.
L400: Study 20200491 provides the primary efficacy evidence to support the proposed indication. Part 1 of this trial is a dose randomization study which showed the overall response rate (ORR) of 40% (95% CI: 31%, 51%) in 1/10 mg cohort and 32% (95% CI: 23%, 41%) in 1/100 mg (at the same dosing schedule as 1/10 mg) with 1/10 mg cohort demonstrated a numerically lower rate of any grade neutropenia and Grade 1 and Grade 2 neurotoxicity including immune effector cell-associated neurotoxicity syndrome (ICANS), supporting the selection of 1/10 mg dosage for Part 2 and Part 3 of Study 20200491.
L401: The key review issues are focused on the evaluation of dose selection, drug interaction management and recommendations for restarting therapy after dose delay. The population pharmacokinetics (popPK) analyses found none of the intrinsic or extrinsic covariates including age (32 to 82 years), bodyweight (35 to 149 kg), sex, race (White and Asian), mild or moderate renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment had a clinically important effect on the pharmacokinetic (PK) of tarlatamab. The exposure-response (E-R) analyses found a positive trend between ORR and exposure with a plateau being achieved at 1/10 mg. A statistically significant positive E-R for safety was also identified for all grade neutropenia and Grade 1 and Grade 2 neurotoxicity including ICANS. The impact of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) on efficacy, safety, and PK of tarlatamab is unknown due to a low ADA incidence of 3%. Transient elevations of circulating cytokines interleukin (IL)2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, and interferon-gamma (INF-\gamma) were observed at dosages starting at 0.3 mg. The integrated popPK, E-R, pharmacodynamics (PD) analyses along with safety, and efficacy from the Study 20200491 and the dose-finding first in human (FIH) study 20160323 support the proposed tarlatamab 1/10 mg dosing regimen.
L402: ## Recommendations
L403: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in BLA 761344. This BLA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below in
L404: Table 13.
L405: **Table 13: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues and Recommendations by FDA**
L406: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from Study 20200491 which demonstrated ORR of 40% (95% CI: 31%, 51%) with the proposed 1/10 mg dosing regimen.
General dosing instructions	The 1/10 mg is selected with the following rationales:The step-up dose 1 mg mitigated the risk of cytokine release syndrome (CRS), especially \ge Grade 2 CRS for the subsequent full doses.
CRS grade and incidence for each dose event did not differ significantly across body weight (BW) quartiles, ages or DLL-3 cut-points.
The 1/10 mg dosage showed a numerically higher ORR than 1/100 mg in Study 20200491.
E-R analyses for efficacy with doses up to 100 mg showed ORR plateaued at the selected 1/10 mg.
E-R analyses for safety did not identify major safety concerns with 1/10 mg. A positive E-R safety trend was observed between tarlatamab exposure and any grade neutropenia, Grade 1 and Grade 2 neurotoxicity.

Dosage recommendations for restarting therapy after dose delay	The proposed dosage recommendation for restarting therapy after dose delay aims to mitigate CRS risk and is supported by:Rebound CRS risk is low and no clear trend for rebound CRS increase with longer dose delays. However, data is limited for longer duration of dose delays.
Based on the totality of data including PK simulation and clinical data, FDA agrees with Applicant's proposed restart therapy recommendation.

Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dosage adjustment is necessary according to BW, age, sex, race (White and Asian), mild to moderate renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment, DLL3 target expression. However, limited data on African
L407: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
American for PK assessment and no data to assess the impact of ethnicity (e.g., Hispanic or Latino) on tarlatamab's PK/PD in the reported clinical trials. See PMC below.
Drug-drug interactions	Tarlatamab causes transient release of cytokines during CRS that may suppress cytochrome (CYP) 450 enzymes and may result in an increased exposure of concomitant CYP substrate; however, based on FDA's evaluation of literature and IL-6 concentrations, the risk of drug interaction for concomitant CYP substrates with tarlatamab appears to be low in the overall patient population. For addition detail, refer section 6.3.1.
Immunogenicity	In Study 20200491, 3.2% (4/124) of patients developed ADA and none of the patients developed neutralizing antibodies (Nab). Because of the low occurrence, the effect of ADA on the PK, PD, safety, and effectiveness of tarlatamab is inconclusive.
Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable upon Applicant's agreement with the FDA recommended labeling revisions.

L412: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L413: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L414: In Study 20160323 and Study 2020491, tarlatamab exhibited approximate dose-proportional increases in exposures in the evaluated dose range of 0.003 to 100 mg IV administered Q2W and 200 mg Q3W. The steady state in tarlatamab serum concentrations were approximately achieved by the start of cycle 2. The mean (SD) estimated terminal phase elimination half-life at steady state was 5.5 (1.6) days across the evaluated dose range.
L415: Following the proposed dosing regimen of 1 mg on day 1 and 10 mg on day 8, day 15, and Q2W thereafter, tarlatamab geometric mean trough values at steady state (cycle 2 day 15 predose) and peak serum concentrations at steady state (cycle 2 day 15, end of infusion) were 0.44 and 4.3 mg/mL, respectively, based on pooled data across phase 1 and phase 2 studies. There was no indication of time dependent kinetics.
L416: Based on population PK analyses, tarlatamab serum clearance, central volume of distribution, peripheral volume of distribution, and volume of distribution at steady state were estimated to be 0.65 L/day, 3.44 L, 5.06 L, and 8.5 L, respectively for a typical 73 kg White individual. The inter-individual variability for tarlatamab clearance and central volume of distribution were 43.7% and 38.7%, respectively.
L417: At the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W, the incidence of treatment-emergent antitarlatamab antibodies was 12% (3 of 25 subjects) for the first-in-human (FIH) Study 20160323 and 3.2% (4 of 124 subjects) for the pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491, for a pooled total of 7 of 149 subjects (4.7%) across the 2 studies. In the pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491, which employed the neutralizing assay, none of the subjects
L418: developed neutralizing antibodies. Potential impact of antitarlatamab antibodies on the PK, clinical efficacy, and clinical safety of tarlatamab was assessed with the following results:
L419: - Based on the population PK analysis, individuals with positive antitarlatamab antibody binding status at any time during the study were estimated to have approximately 21% higher clearance, which translated to an approximately 24% decrease in serum trough concentrations on average. However, this difference in exposures did not have a clinically relevant impact on efficacy.
- At the proposed dosing regimen, the efficacy measures (including ORR, disease control rate [DCR], and DOR) were similar for antitarlatamab binding antibody positive subjects compared with those who were antitarlatamab antibody negative.
- There was no apparent impact of antitarlatamab antibody development on safety including adverse events.
L420: A detailed description of these analyses can be found in the Integrated Summary of Immunogenicity (Module 5.3.5.3).
L421: ## The Applicant's Position:
L422: In both Studies 20160232 and 20200491, tarlatamab exhibited approximate dose-proportional increases in exposures in the evaluated dose range. The steady state in tarlatamab serum concentrations were approximately achieved by the start of cycle 2. The mean (SD) estimated terminal phase elimination half-life at steady state was 5.5 (1.6) days, across the evaluated dose range. The incidence of antitarlatamab antibody development was low. There was no meaningful clinical impact of antitarlatamab antibody development on efficacy or safety.
L423: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L424: The clinical pharmacology program included data from Studies 20160232 and 20200491. FDA agrees that the results of these studies generally provide adequate characterization of tarlatamab PK properties as described below and the PK parameters are presented as geometric mean (CV%) unless otherwise specified:
L425: Dose proportionality: The exposure of tarlatamab increased dose proportionally in the evaluated dose range of 1 mg to 100 mg.
L426: Distribution: Tarlatamab's steady state volume of distribution is 8.6 L (18.3%).
L427: Elimination: Tarlatamab's median elimination half-life (min, max) is 11.2 (4.3 to 26.5) days, and the estimated clearance is 0.65 L/day (44%) in patients with SCLC. Steady state was achieved by Cycle 2 Day 15.
L428: Immunogenicity: 3 % (4/214) of patients tested positive for ADA and none of the patients tested positive for nAb in the pivotal Study 20200491.
L429: Refer to section 6.3.1 and 19.4.1 for details.
L430: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L431: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L432: ## Data:
L433: The proposed dose and dosing regimen for tarlatamab for the treatment of previously treated advanced SCLC is an initial 1 mg dose on cycle 1 day 1, followed by 10 mg on cycle 1 day 8, cycle 1 day 15, and Q2W thereafter, all doses administered via a 1-hour IV infusion. The totality of exposure-response data from the dose exploration and expansion parts of the phase 1 FIH Study 20160323 (dose range 0.003 to 100 mg Q2W and 200 mg Q3W) supported the selection of 2 dose levels of 10 and 100 mg Q2W for further assessment in a randomized manner in Part 1 of the pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491. The analyses from the FIH Study 20160323 demonstrated numerically higher efficacy to be associated with 10 mg exposures relative to lower doses (1 or 3 mg). The analysis also displayed numerical benefit in DCR with exposures associated with the 100 mg Q2W regimen relative to the 10 mg Q2W regimen, therefore supporting the selection of 100 mg as the higher dose level (in addition to 10 mg) to be evaluated in pivotal Study 20200491. Importantly, from a safety and tolerability perspective, a maximal tolerated dose level was not achieved in the ongoing FIH Study 20160323, within the evaluated dose range.
L434: Of the 2 dose levels assessed in Part 1 of pivotal Study 20200491, the 10 mg Q2W regimen was selected at the prespecified interim analysis for further evaluation in Part 2. At the time of interim analysis, measures of efficacy response were similar between the 10 and 100 mg groups. In the 10 mg group, the confirmed ORR (assessed by the investigator) was 34.4% (95% CI: 18.6, 53.2) and the DCR was 75.0% (95% CI: 56.6, 88.5). In the 100 mg group, the confirmed ORR was 35.5% (95% CI: 19.2, 54.6), and the DCR was 64.5% (95% CI: 45.4, 80.8) (Table 12 of Rationale for Recommended Phase 2 Dose [RP2D] (Module 5.3.5.2). At the time of the interim analysis, compared with the 100 mg group, subjects in the 10 mg group experienced fewer treatment-emergent adverse events (Table 19 of Rationale for RP2D). Consistent with the dose-response data, exposure-response analyses demonstrated that exposures with the 10 mg dose regimen were associated with improved tolerability but comparable efficacy relative to the 100 mg dose. Further details of the data available and analysis results at the time of prespecified interim analysis are provided in the Rationale for RP2D (Module 5.3.5.2).
L435: To confirm the selection of 10 mg Q2W regimen, these exposure-response analyses were updated using pooled data from Study 20160323 and Study 20200491 based on the latest cutoff dates of 28 March 2023 and 27 June 2023, respectively. These analyses further confirmed that the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W exhibited near maximal efficacy and favorable benefit-risk profile in the intended patient population.
L436: ## The Applicant's Position:
L437: Overall, results of exposure-response analysis suggest the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W exhibited near maximal efficacy with a limited impact on neutropenia and favorable benefit-risk profile in the intended patient population. None of the covariates evaluated in this analysis resulted in a clinically meaningful change in efficacy or safety and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.
L438: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L439: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on dose selection of 1/10 mg based on efficacy and safety data from Studies 20200491 and 20160232.
L440: The proposed 1 mg step-dose regimen appears to be acceptable based on the following assessment:
L441: The risk of CRS of an alternative step-up dose for 10 mg target doses is unknown as no alternative step-up regimen was investigated. In Study 20160232, 1 mg step-up was investigated for all target dosages starting at 3 mg. The risk of Grade 2 or above CRS occurrence appeared to be low (\le 30\%) at all dosages (Table 14). The 1 mg step-up was further evaluated in Study 20200491.
L442: Following administration of 1/10 mg in Study 20200491, in the primary safety population, the majority of CRS incidences were Grade 1 (30%), with the highest rate (40%) observed following the step-up dose 1 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 (
L443: Table 15 and Figure 3). Grade 3 CRS occurred less frequently in the 1/10 mg cohort (1%) than in the 1/100-mg cohort (6%). The risk of CRS in the subsequent cycles of the 10 mg target doses was mitigated by the initial 1 mg step-up dose. Majority of CRS had a fast onset of 11 to 25 hours, subsequent to 1 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 and 10 mg on Cycle 1 Days 8. According to the Sunburst plot (Figure 5), incidences of late onset CRS were low. Thirteen patients (9%) who did not experience CRS following the step-up dose 1 mg, developed CRS following the first treatment dose 10 mg on Cycle 1 Day 8; one patient (1%) developed CRS following the second treatment dose on Cycle 1 Day 15 among the patients who did not experience CRS after step-up dose and first treatment dose. For 1/10 mg cohort, a few patients' treatment discontinuation(n=3) and dose interruption (n=4) events might be attributable to CRS occurrence and labeled as no dose events. Other no dose events in Figure 5 and Figure 3 were attributable to other AEs, patient withdrawal and disease progression. Overall, there is no significant CRS concern identified with the 1 mg step-up dose for the 10 mg treatment doses.
L444: In Study 20200491, 1/100 mg dosage resulted in a higher overall CRS rate of 63% and a different CRS dynamics with the highest CRS (49%) including Grade 2 (13%) and Grade 3 (5%) observed following the 100 mg treatment dose on Cycle 1 Day 8 (Figure 4 and
L445: Table 15). This may be due to immune system is not sufficiently stimulated during the 1 mg step-up dose for the 100 mg treatment dose.

L473: - The efficacy data from Study 20160323 indicated that dosages \ge 3 mg are active (Table 14). L477: Consistent with dose-response (D-R) data in Study 20200491, E-R analyses for efficacy suggested a higher tarlatamab exposure may associate with a higher ORR rate; however, a plateau of efficacy was reached at the exposure of 1/10 mg, supporting the proposed dosage (Figure 6).

L479: Cavg = first cycle model-estimated averaged concentration over the first cycle; ORR = objective response rate. Source: Figure 8 of Exposure-response Analysis Report
L480: 3) The E-R safety analyses did not identify major safety concerns with the proposed dosage. Positive E-R for safety trends were observed for any grade neutropenia, grade 1 and grade 2 neurotoxicity including ICANs, supporting the lower dosage 1/10 mg. A positive trend was also observed for Grade \ge 1 CRS and tarlatamab exposure; however, this trend is not conclusive and may be confounded by the effect of step-up dose to subsequent treatment doses. No trends of E-R for safety were identified for Grade 3 AEs, Grade 3 TEAEs, or Grade \ge2 CRS. There were limited data for other AEs. Refer to Appendix 19.4.4 for detailed E-R for safety analyses.
L481: Efficacy and safety data from Study 20200491 together with the E-R analyses for safety and efficacy support the recommended dosage 1/10 mg in patient with SCLC.
L482: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L483: ## **Data:**
L484: The influence of demographic and subject specific characteristics on the PK of tarlatamab were investigated using population PK. Various demographic variables were evaluated in the covariate testing within the population PK analyses framework. Key demographic factors (age, sex, ethnicity), estimates of renal function and liver function, number of prior lines of therapies, baseline disease burden or Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) status, did not affect tarlatamab PK.
L485: Consistent with other biologics, body weight was correlated with tarlatamab clearance, as was positive binding ADA status with increase in body weight or positive ADA binding status associated with increase in clearance. However, at the proposed dosing regimen of 1 mg on day 1 followed by 10 mg on day 8, day 15, and Q2W thereafter, neither body weight nor ADA status had a clinically relevant impact on tarlatamab efficacy as assessed by confirmed ORR or DCR. Race (Asian/Black/Others vs White) was found to be correlated with central volume of distribution; however, race had a negligible impact on exposures. The exposure-response relationship for ORR, DCR, best tumor size response (BTSR), and PFS was not impacted by evaluated intrinsic or extrinsic factors (see Section 6.3.2 and Section 3.5 of Module 2.7.2, Summary of Clinical Pharmacology).
L486: Exposure-response relationships for key efficacy measures (ORR, DCR, BTSR, and PFS) or safety measures were not impacted by any of the evaluated covariates (see Section 6.3.2). Higher sum of tumor lesion diameter at baseline (independent of tarlatamab serum exposures) was significantly associated with higher hazard for OS.
L487: Detailed results are provided in Report 157688 (Population PK Report) (Module 5.3.3.5) and Report 157687 (Exposure-response Analysis Report) (Module 5.3.4.2).
L488: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L489: None of the covariates evaluated in this analysis resulted in clinically meaningful changes in efficacy or safety and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.
L490: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L491: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose adjustment for tarlatamab is needed for intrinsic and extrinsic factors including age, BW, sex, race (White and Asian), mild to moderate renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment, as the popPK analyses showed no clinically important effect of these covariates on the PK or E-R efficacy relationship of tarlatamab. Due to limited data, the effects of severe renal impairment, end-stage renal disease, moderate to severe hepatic impairment, ADA, or ethnicity (e.g., Hispanic or Latino) on tarlatamab PK are unknown.
L492: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that E-R relationships for efficacy and safety
L493: support proposed 1/10 mg dosage. E-R relationship for efficacy suggested a potential trend of higher tarlatamab exposure with higher ORR with responses plateaued at 1/10 mg. Positive E-R for safety trends were observed for any grade neutropenia, Grade 1 and Grade 2 neurotoxicity including ICANs, supporting the lower dosage 1/10 mg. The E-R relationships for safety did not identify major safety concerns on the 1/10 mg dosage as no clear trends were identified for grade \ge 3 AEs of interest.
L494: Despite a trend toward higher DLL3 H-scores in patients who responded to both the 1/10 and 1/100 mg dosages, partial response observed in a patient lacking DLL3 target expression (H-score=0) supports treatment of patients irrespective of baseline DLL3 expression status.
L495: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L496: ## Data:
L497: Not applicable
L498: ## The Applicant's Position:
L499: None
L500: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L501: There is limited PK data to assess the impact of PK differences relating to race (e.g., African American) or ethnicity (e.g., Hispanic or Latino) minorities. As described in the PMC that will be issued with the Approval letter, the Applicant should conduct an integrated analysis of data from clinical trial(s) to further characterize the safety, efficacy, PK of tarlatamab among US racial and ethnic minority patients with SCLC. The population should be representative of US population.
L502: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L503: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L504: ## Data:
L505: ## Pharmacokinetics
L506: Tarlatamab exhibited approximate dose-proportional increases in exposures in the evaluated dose range of 0.003 to 100 mg IV administered Q2W and 200 mg Q3W. The
L507: mean (SD) estimated terminal phase elimination half-life at steady state was 5.5 (1.6) days across the evaluated dose range.
L508: Following the proposed dosing regimen of 1 mg on day 1 and 10 mg on day 8, day 15, and Q2W thereafter, tarlatamab geometric mean trough values at steady state (cycle 2 day 15 predose) and peak serum concentrations at steady state (cycle 2 day 15, end of infusion) were 0.44 and 4.3 mg/mL, respectively, based on pooled data across phase 1 and phase 2 studies. There was no indication of time dependent kinetics.
L509: Based on population PK analyses, tarlatamab serum clearance, central volume of distribution, peripheral volume of distribution, and volume of distribution at steady state were estimated to be 0.65 L/day, 3.44 L, 5.06 L, and 8.5 L, respectively for a typical 73 kg White individual. The inter-individual variability for tarlatamab clearance and central volume of distribution were 43.7% and 38.7%, respectively.
L510: Various demographic variables were evaluated in the covariate testing within the population PK analyses framework. Key demographic factors (age, sex, ethnicity), estimates of renal function and liver function, number of prior lines of therapies, baseline disease burden or ECOG status, did not affect tarlatamab PK.
L511: Consistent with other biologics, body weight was correlated with tarlatamab clearance, as was positive binding ADA status with increase in body weight or positive ADA binding status associated with increase in clearance. However, at the proposed dosing regimen of 1 mg on day 1 followed by 10 mg on day 8, day 15, and Q2W thereafter, neither body weight nor ADA status had a clinically relevant impact on tarlatamab efficacy as assessed by confirmed ORR or DCR. Race was found to be correlated with central volume of distribution; however, race had a negligible impact on exposures.
L512: At the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W, the incidence of treatment-emergent antitarlatamab antibodies was 12% (3 of 25 subjects) for the FIH Study 20160323 and 3.2% (4 of 124 subjects) for the pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491, for a pooled total of 7 of 149 subjects (4.7%) across the 2 studies. In the pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491, which employed the neutralizing assay, none of the subjects developed neutralizing antibodies. Potential impact of antitarlatamab antibodies on the PK, clinical efficacy, and clinical safety of tarlatamab was assessed with the following results:
L513: - Based on the population PK analysis, individuals with positive antitarlatamab antibody binding status at any time during the study were estimated to have approximately 21% higher clearance, which translated to an approximately 24% decrease in serum trough concentrations on average. However, this difference in exposures did not have a clinically relevant impact on efficacy.
- At the proposed dosing regimen, the efficacy measures (including ORR, DCR, and DOR) were comparable for antitarlatamab binding antibody positive subjects compared with those who were antitarlatamab antibody negative.
- There was no apparent impact of antitarlatamab antibody development on safety based on assessment of adverse events.
L514: Detailed description of these analyses can be found in the Integrated Summary of Immunogenicity (Module 5.3.5.3).
L515: ## The Applicant's Position:
L516: In both Studies 20160232 and 20200491, tarlatamab exhibited approximate dose-proportional increases in exposures in the evaluated dose range. The steady state in tarlatamab serum concentrations were approximately achieved by the start of cycle 2. The mean (SD) estimated terminal phase elimination half-life at steady state was 5.5 (1.6) days, across the evaluated dose range. The incidence of antitarlatamab antibody development was low. There was no meaningful clinical impact of antitarlatamab antibody development on efficacy and safety.
L517: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L518: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position regarding the characteristics of clinical pharmacology and pharmacokinetics of tarlatamab as summarized in Table 16. The PK parameters are presented as geometric mean (CV%) unless otherwise specified.
L519: **Table 16: Tarlatamab General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics of Tarlatamab Summarized by FDA.**
L520: General Information
PK Bioanalytical assay	An electrochemiluminescent (ECL) assay was used for PK assay. The method validation and in-study performance for plasma samples are acceptable based on the current Bioanalytical Method Validation Draft FDA Guidance for Industry. Refer to section 19.5 for review of analytical method validation.
PK Characteristics
Steady-state exposure	Steady state in serum exposures were achieved by approximately Cycle 2 Day 15. See Figure 7 and Table 17 for predicted steady-state concentrations and profiles, respectively.
Dose proportionality	Tarlatamab exposure increased dose proportionally in the evaluated dose range of 1mg to 100 mg every 2 week
Distribution	Tarlatamab steady state volume of distribution is 8.6 L (18.3%)
Elimination	Tarlatamab's median terminal half-life (min, max) is 11.2 (4.3 to
L521: 26.5) days, and the estimated clearance is 0.65 L/day (44%) in patients with SCLC.
Specific Population
Intrinsic Factor	
No clinically meaningful differences in the PK were observed based on age (32 to 82 years), BW (35 to 149 kg), sex, race (White and Asian), mild to moderate renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment. Although BW and race were identified as statistically significant covariates on tarlatamab volume of distribution and clearance, the impacts of these covariates on tarlatamab exposure were not considered clinically relevant. Positive ADA was also identified as a significant time-varying covariate on tarlatamab clearance. Due to low occurrence of ADA, the clinically relevant effect of ADA on tarlatamab's PK, efficacy and safety is not conclusive. None of the covariates evaluated above found to have important effect on E-R relationships for efficacy. Therefore, no dose adjustment is needed based on these intrinsic or extrinsic covariates.
No difference in ORR was observed at the DLL3 cut points of < 75% vs ≥ 75% and < 25% vs ≥ 25% at moderate (2+) and strong (3+) staining intensities (see section 8.1.6). These results support treatment of patients at 1/10 mg irrespective of baseline DLL3 expression status.
The effects of severe renal impairment, end-stage renal disease, moderate to severe hepatic impairment, ADA, or ethnicity (e.g., Hispanic or Latino) are unknown due to limited or unavailable data.

PD Characteristics
Pharmacodynamics	
Transient elevation of serum cytokines IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, and IFN-\gamma were observed at a tarlatamab dosage of 0.3 mg Q2W and above. Peak elevation of cytokines was generally observed 20-24 hours following the initial dose of tarlatamab at 1 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 and generally returned to baseline levels prior to the next infusion on Cycle 1 Day 8.

Exposure-Response for	
Higher exposures (e.g., C_{\text{avg, first cycle}}) were associated with higher ORR. The efficacy reached a plateau at the higher exposures of target dosage 10 mg Q2W supporting the proposed dosage.
L522: Efficacy	
Exposure-Response for Safety	Higher tarlatamab exposures (e.g., C_{avg,first\ cycle}) were associated with higher incidences of any grade neutropenia, grade 1 and grade 2 neurotoxicity including ICANs. E-R for safety analysis did not identify major safety concerns for the proposed dosage.

L531: ## Cytokine levels
L532: FDA assessed the potential for drug interactions between tarlatamab and CYP substrates and considered this risk is low. In Study 2020049, cytokine levels were transiently elevated and mostly peaked around 20-24 hours after step-up dose (1 mg) on Cycle 1 Day 1 and returning to baseline around the next target dose (10 mg) on Cycle 1 Day 8 (Figure 8 and Table 18). The concentrations of IL-6 for the total samples, samples without tocilizumab and samples with tocilizumab were summarized in Table 18.
L533: Prior literature and validated physiologically based pharmacokinetic model (PBPK) derived the EC50 values for IL-6 on CYP3A4 and CYP3A5 to be 75.2 pg/mL and 51 pg/mL, respectively (Jiang 2016). The median and mean value of peak IL-6 concentrations are 26 pg/mL (Table 18). This peak concentration did not exceed the literature reported EC50 of IL-6, suggesting a clinically relevant interaction between tarlatamab and CYP3A substrates is not expected, and the mean value of IL-6 was lower for tarlatamab than other bispecific drugs including elranatamab and epcoritamab (Table 19). Based on the predicted low drug interaction risk in the general patient population, the information is not included in Section 7 of the USPI.
L534: Figure 10 showed an exploratory analysis, in patients who developed Grade 2 CRS, the peak IL-6 may exceed the EC50 and could potentially lead to suppression of CYP3A
L535: activity. The risk of drug interaction during and after any CRS cannot be ruled out. Therefore, FDA provided additional language in Section 12 of the USPI that tarlatamab may increase exposure of concomitant CYP substrates during and up to 14 days after occurrence of CRS.
L536: Cytokine release profiles including INF-\gamma, IL-2, IL-5, IL-10, and tumor necrosis factor alpha (TNF-alpha) in peripheral blood over time without tocilizumab use in Study 2020049 as displayed in Figure 9. Similar time course of cytokine concentrations was also observed for other cytokines except for TNF-alpha. TNF-alpha did not change significantly from the baseline and median concentrations were consistently below 10 pg/mL. For additional details, refer to section 19.4.2.

L561: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L562: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L563: ## **Data:**
L564: Data from Study 20200491 (phase 2) and Study 20160323 (FIH) were included in an integrated exposure-response analysis to support the selection of the proposed dosing regimen (summarized in Section 3.4 of the Clinical Overview and detailed in Section 3.5 of Module 2.7.2, Summary of Clinical Pharmacology). Relationships between tarlatamab exposure and selected measures of response (ORR, DCR, BTSR, PFS, OS, and DOR) were evaluated. The tarlatamab exposure metrics evaluated as predictors of response were model-predicted average concentrations over the first cycle (C_{avg}), peak serum concentrations over the first cycle (C_{max}), and trough serum concentrations at the end of the first cycle on day 28 (C_{trough}), generated using a population PK model. Covariates included in the analyses were demographic factors (age, body weight, sex, race, ethnicity), organ impairment (normal, mild, moderate renal and hepatic function), baseline serum albumin, disease status (ECOG and sum of tumor lesion diameters at baseline), number of prior therapies, and positive ADA status.
L565: With regards to evidence of effectiveness, integrated analysis using pooled data from Study 20160323 and Study 20200491 show that:
L566: - Significant exposure-response relationships were established for key efficacy measures including ORR, DCR, BTSR, PFS, and OS.
- Near maximal efficacy was observed at exposures associated with the proposed clinical regimen of 10 mg Q2W.
- Exposures associated with lower doses are estimated to have lower efficacy.
L567: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L568: Overall, results of exposure-response analysis for efficacy measures suggest the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W exhibited near maximal efficacy in the intended patient population, and exposures associated with lower doses are estimated to have lower efficacy. None of the covariates evaluated in this analysis resulted in clinically meaningful changes in efficacy and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.
L569: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L570: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position on that E-R analyses, in addition to the clinical findings from Studies 20200491 and 20160323, supported the proposed 1/10 mg dosing regimen. In Study 20160323 dose escalation, 0.003 to 100 mg Q2W were explored.
L571: Efficacy analyses suggested a higher response may associate with higher target dosages. 1/10 mg and 1/100 mg were then selected to be evaluated in part I dose randomization study of 20200491. The 1/10 mg demonstrated a numerically higher ORR relative to 1/100 mg in Study 20200491. E-R efficacy suggested a potential trend of higher tarlatamab exposure associated with higher ORR, which plateaued at the 1/10 mg, supporting the proposed dose. Positive E-R for safety trends were observed for any grade neutropenia, Grade 1 and Grade 2 neurotoxicity including ICANS, supporting the lower dosage 1/10 mg.
L572: The 1 mg step-up dose was investigated for all dosages starting at 3 mg and the risk of CRS of an alternative step-up dose for 10 mg is unknown. In Study 20200491, 1 mg step-up dose resulted in more favorable CRS profiles with overall grade CRS occurred primarily after first step-up dose (40%) and first full dose (28%) and only 2% of CRS events for third full dose. Overall, based on a lower incidence of grade \ge 2 CRS, as well as the favorable CRS dynamic, the step-up dose appeared to be reasonable.
L573: Refer to section 6.2.2.1 for details regarding justification for the step-up doses and full doses and refer to section 19.4.4 for details regarding the exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety.
L574: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L575: ## Data:
L576: See Section 6.2.2.1, General Dosing
L577: The proposed dose is effective and generally well tolerated in subjects with advanced SCLC. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the observed efficacy and safety data and demonstrates a favorable benefit-risk profile. In addition to exposure-response analysis for efficacy, analysis correlating tarlatamab exposures with key safety measures using pooled data from Study 20160323 and Study 20200491 were performed. No significant exposure-safety relationships for grade \ge 3 adverse events, grade \ge 3 treatment-related adverse events, or grade \ge 3 adverse events of interest (neurological events, immune effector cell-associated neurotoxicity syndrome [ICANS], and cytokine release syndrome [CRS]) were identified. A shallow trend was observed for higher percentage of subjects experiencing grade \ge 3 neutropenia with increasing tarlatamab exposures. In addition, no clinically relevant impact on QTc interval was observed at the proposed clinical regimen of 10 mg Q2W.
L578: ## The Applicant's Position:
L579: Overall, results of exposure-response analysis suggest the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W exhibited near maximal efficacy with a limited impact on neutropenia and favorable benefit-risk profile in the intended patient population. None of the covariates
L580: evaluated in this analysis resulted in clinically meaningful changes in efficacy or safety and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.
L581: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L582: FDA agrees with Applicants' position that the proposed dosage 1/10 mg tarlatamab is acceptable for the patient population for which the indication is being sought. See detailed discussion in section Error! Reference source not found.. FDA agrees that no clinically significant QT prolongation was observed, this is consistent with tarlatamab being a protein therapeutic with a molecular weight of 105 KDa, which is not expected to inhibit human ether-a-go-go related gene (hERG) channel function directly.
L583: APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL
L584: ## **6.3.2.1 Dosage Recommendations for Restarting Therapy after Dose Delay**
L585: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L586: FDA agrees with Applicant's proposed recommendations for restarting therapy after dose delay, as outlined in Table 21, based on clinical data and PK simulations. Although CRS associated with tarlatamab treatment occurred primarily with the first dose, risk of CRS re-emerged when a patient was off treatment for a prolonged period. This is due to re-sensitization of the immune system and potential rebound of the target cells. Therefore, repeating step-up dose 1 mg at the proposed cutoffs following dose delays is needed to mitigate the risk of rebound CRS.
L587: **Table 21. Recommendations for Restarting Therapy After Dosage Delay**
L588: Last Dose Administered	Time Since the Last Dose Administered	Actiona

Greater than 2 weeks (>14 days)	Administer IMDELLTRA step-up dose 1 mg. If tolerated, increase to 10 mg 1 week later. Then resume with the planned dosage schedule.
10 mg Cycle 1
Day 8	3 weeks or less (≤21 days)	Administer IMDELLTRA 10 mg, then resume with the planned dosage schedule.
Greater than 3 weeks (>21 days)	Administer IMDELLTRA step-up dose 1 mg. If tolerated, increase to 10 mg 1 week later. Then resume with the planned dosage schedule.

onward	4 weeks or less (\le 28 days)	Administer IMDELLTRA 10 mg, then resume with the planned dosage schedule.

L591: Source: Table 2, Tarlatamab USPI.
L592: The proposed restart recommendations after dose delay were implemented in Study 20200491. In Study 20200491, there were 6 episodes of repriming with 1 mg step-up dose: 2 episodes of &gt; 21 days of dose delays following the 1 mg step-up dose, 2 episodes of &gt; 28 days of dose delay follow the first full dose of 10 mg on Day 8, and 2 episodes of &gt; 36 days of dose delay following third and later 10 mg Q2W. No CRS was reported in any of these episodes of repriming.
L593: FDA's assessment on the adequacy of the proposed recommendations for restarting therapy after dose delay, as detailed below, include evaluations of observed rebound CRS data after dose delay and PK simulations following different dosing delay scenarios. In Table 22Table 27, evaluation of rebound CRS events following dose delay in 20200491 indicated that no increase in the risk of CRS following dose delays that were within the proposed cut-off for repriming and no patients experienced Grade 2 or higher CRS upon the resumption of treatment. The overall risk of rebound CRS is assessed to be low. However, there is sparse data to support evaluation of longer dose delay intervals greater than proposed cut-off for repriming. There were three patients experienced dose delays ranging from 15 to 21 days following the first step-up dose with no CRS, a Grade 1 CRS and a Grade 2 CRS, respectively. One patient had dose delay ranging from 22 to 28 days following the first treatment dose and five patients had dose delays greater than 28 days following the subsequent full treatment dose. None of these patients developed rebound CRS. The overall clinical data is not concerning regarding rebound CRS or against the proposed re-treatment plan.
L594: Table 22. FDA –Summary of the CRS Events following Dose Delays in Study 20200491.

L604: PK simulation
L605: Due to limited clinical data on dose delay, the dosage recommendation for restarting doses or repeating doses were assessed based on a PK simulation. Tarlatamab exposure after each dose delay scenario was simulated with the popPK model and compared to the reference, tarlatamab exposure after the recommended schedules without delays. From a PK perspective, the C_{\text{trough}} at the maximum allowed dose delay interval should be similar (20%) or higher than the C_{\text{trough}} for next lower dose level (e.g., the step-up doses) to avoid risk of rebound CRS.
L606: **Table 24: Simulated Tarlatamab Median C_{\text{trough}} for Dose Delay Scenarios**
L607: Last Dose Administered	Time Since the Last Dose Administered	Action for Next Dose	Simulated C_{\text{trough}} (ng/mL) at End of Dose Delay	Reference C_{\text{trough}} (ng/mL)
Step-up dose 1 (1 mg) on day 1	2 weeks or less (\le 14 days)	Continue at 10 mg	30 (10-48)	51(24-71)
First Full Dose (10 mg) on day 8	3 weeks or less (\le 21 days)	Continue at 10 mg	204 (50-375)	51(24-71)
10 mg day 15 and onward and Q2W	4 weeks or less (\le 28 days)	Continue at 10 mg	208 (36.4-461)	51(24-71)
L608: Median and 90% prediction interval are reported; C_{\text{trough}} = trough concentration; Q2W = every 2 weeks
*Source: Applicant's response to FDA IR on 1/3/2024*
L609: The simulated C_{\text{trough}} was higher than reference except in the first dose delay scenario (Table 24). The predicted C_{\text{trough}} following the first step-up dose C1D1 dose delay for 14 days was approximately 59% of the reference value. Figure 11 shows when patients received 1 mg on Day 1 and followed by dose interruption, under with and without repriming scenario. Although the initial C_{\text{trough}} followed by the dose interruption is lower than the reference, the concentration reaches to that of reference once next target dose is administered. The initial drop in the concentration is not likely to be clinically relevant. The proposed 14 days dose delay cutoff was further supported by safety data observed in study 20160323. During the dose escalation phase, cohort 6 in the dose escalation evaluated the dosing regimen of 1 mg Q2W in 8 patients without a step dose. Overall, CRS was lower than 1/10 mg cohorts, supporting the 14-day cutoff before repriming in scenario 1 (Table 14).
L610: Based on the simulated PK profile and available clinical data, FDA agrees proceeding with 10 mg doses when the dose interruption \le 14 days following the 1 mg step-up dose.
L611: **Figure 11. Simulated median PK profiles – Reference (Planned Clinical Dosing Regimen) compared to dosing delay after step-up dose 1 with and without re-priming.**
L612: The line displayed median and shaded area represented the 90% prediction interval. The red line display simulation of planned clinical dosing regimen, which is the reference concentration. The green line displays delayed dosing, re-priming not required (e.g., Dose 1 step-up 1 mg, Day 15 target dose 10 mg, Day 22 target dose 10 mg, followed by planned dosing schedule). The blue line displays dosing, re-priming required, repeat step-dose schedule per Cycle 1 (e.g., simulation of step-up dose 1 1mg, day 16 step up dose 1mg, day 23 target dose 10mg, day 30 target dose 10mg followed by planned dosing schedule).

L614: ## 6.3.2.2 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors (e.g. race, ethnicity, age, performance status, genetic subpopulations, etc.)?
L615: ## Data:
L616: See Section 6.2.2.2, Therapeutic Individualization.
L617: ## The Applicant's Position:
L618: None of the covariates evaluated in these analyses resulted in clinically meaningful changes in efficacy or safety and therefore no dose adjustment is recommended.
L619: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L620: FDA agrees with Applicant's position that there is no need for dose adjustment for the currently proposed indication based on age, BW, sex, race (White and Asian), mild or moderate renal impairment or mild hepatic impairment.
L621: CRS incidence or severity were generally similar across different BW quartiles following the proposed 1/10 mg tarlatamab (Figure 45 Cytokine Release Syndrome Incidences at C1D1, C1D8, C1D15, C2+ with and without Tocilizumab Stratified by Body Weight Following Administration of 1/10 mg Tarlatamab. **Error! Reference source not found.**). Similarly, there is no clear trend of CRS incidence or severity by age quartiles or DLL3 cut points following the proposed dose (Figure 46, and Figure 47). Therefore, FDA agrees that no step-up dose adjustment is needed in the specific sub-population.

ORIGINAL
L623: Due to limited or no available data, the effects of severe renal impairment (eGFR 15 to 29 mL/min), end-stage renal disease (eGFR &lt;15 mL/min), moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt;1.5 times ULN and any AST), ADA, or ethnicity (e.g., Hispanic or Latino) on tarlatamab PK are unknown.
L624: ## **DLL3 expression status**
L625: In study 20200491, DLL3 expression was evaluated by immunohistochemistry (IHC) at baseline in fresh or archival tumor tissue samples. For patients with response assessments determined by BICR (N=185), DLL3 expression data was available for 83% of patients (82/99) in the 1/10 mg cohort and 86% of patients (74/86) in the 1/100 mg cohort. Overall response rates in the subset of patients with DLL3 expression data available were consistent with response rates observed in the full Safety Analysis Set for Part 1 and Part 2 (see section 8.1.2). Although there was a general trend toward higher DLL3 H-scores in patients who responded (Figure 12), 1 patient in the 1/10 mg cohort with an H-score of 0 achieved a PR. Additionally, no difference in ORR was observed at the DLL3 cut-points of &lt; 75% vs ≥ 75% and &lt; 25% vs ≥ 25% at moderate (2+) and strong (3+) staining intensities, as assessed by the Applicant (see section 8.1.6). These results support treatment of patients irrespective of baseline DLL3 expression status.
L626: **Figure 12. FDA – DLL3 H-score by Best Overall Response per BICR in the 1/10 MG and 1/100 MG Cohorts (Subset of Safety Analysis Set for Part 1 and Part 2 with Available DLL3 H-score Results)**

L629: ## 6.3.2.3 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L630: ## Data:
L631: Based on molecular size and route of administration of tarlatamab, no formal food-drug or drug-drug interaction studies were required and therefore not performed.
L632: ## The Applicant's Position:
L633: Not applicable
L634: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L635: FDA agrees with Applicant on that a food effect is not anticipated given tarlatamab is a large molecule and is administered intravenously.
L636: FDA agrees with Applicant's position on that no formal drug-drug interaction studies are required. Based on FDA's evaluation of literature and understanding of postulated mechanism of IL-6 mediated CYP suppression, the drug interaction risk between CYP substrates and tarlatamab is low. Cytokine levels peaked 24 hours after the tarlatamab first dose administration with peak median concentration around 26 pg/mL. However, the risk of drug interaction during and after any CRS cannot be ruled out. Therefore, additional labeling language is provided in Section 12.3 of USPI that tarlatamab may increase exposure of concomitant CYP substrates during and up to 14 days after occurrence of CRS. Refer to section 6.3.1 and 19.4.2 Error! Reference source not found. for review of the cytokine analyses.

L675: ## **The Applicant's Position:**

L677: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L678: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Results from Parts 1 and 2 of Study 20200491 comprise the primary efficacy population to support efficacy evaluation of this application. Efficacy data from Part 3 of Study 20200491 and from patients who received the proposed tarlatamab dose from Study 20160323 were considered as supportive data for efficacy.
L679: Results from Study 20200491 (n=159) and 20160323 (n=27) comprise the primary safety population use to support he safety evaluation of this Application.
L680: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L681: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L682: ## 8.1.1. Pivotal Study 20200491
L683: ## Trial Design
L684: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L685: **Study 20200491** is an ongoing phase 2, open-label, registrational study evaluating tarlatamab for the treatment of subjects with recurrent SCLC who had progressed or recurred following 1 platinum-based regimen (with or without immune checkpoint inhibitor) and at least 1 other line of therapy (retreatment with a platinum-based regimen was considered a second line of therapy) (Figure 13).
L686: This study was conducted in 3 parts. Part 1 evaluated 2 dose levels of tarlatamab in which approximately 180 eligible subjects were planned to be enrolled and randomized 1:1 to either treatment with a target dose of 10 or 100 mg tarlatamab. To select a dose for expansion, an interim analysis was performed after 30 subjects in each group in Part 1 had opportunity to confirm an objective response after the first posttreatment scan, or up to 13 weeks of follow-up, whichever occurred first. Enrollment continued in a randomized fashion until dose selection was finalized. Part 2 was a dose expansion phase at the selected dose based on the interim
L687: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L688: analysis of Part 1 and enrolled until approximately 100 subjects (from Parts 1 and 2 combined) had been enrolled at the selected dose level. Part 3 was initiated after completing enrollment of Parts 1 and 2, to enroll up to approximately 30 additional subjects at the selected dose, with modified cycle 1 monitoring criteria.
L689: **Figure 13. Applicant – Schema of Study 20200491**
L690: *Once 30 subjects per dose level had the opportunity to confirm an objective response after the first post-treatment scan or 3 13 weeks of follow-up, whichever occurred first.
L691: BICR = blinded independent central review; DCR = disease control rate; DOR = duration of response; ECOG PS = Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group performance status; ES-SCLC = extensive-stage small cell lung cancer; ITT = intention-to-treat; ORR = objective response rate; OS = overall survival; PFS = progression-free survival; Q2W = every 2 weeks; R = randomization; RECIST = Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors; TEAE = treatment-emergent adverse event
L692: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L693: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of the trial design of Study 20200491.
L694: ## **Eligibility Criteria**
L695: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L696: ## **Study 20200491**
L697: Eligible subjects were men or women (\ge 18 years of age [or legal adult age within country]) with histologically or cytologically confirmed relapsed or refractory SCLC who progressed or recurred following 1 platinum-based regimen and at least 1 other prior line of therapy (Note: [1] re-treatment with a platinum-based regimen was considered a second line of therapy; [2] platinum-based regimen followed by immune checkpoint/PD-L1 inhibitor as maintenance
L698: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L699: therapy was considered 1 line of therapy; [3] in countries where standard of care first-line systemic treatment included platinum-containing chemotherapy in combination with PD-L1 inhibitor, it was required for subjects to have failed or be ineligible for PD-L1 inhibitor as part of their first-line systemic treatment).
L700: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L701: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of key eligibility criteria for Study 20200491. Additional key inclusion criteria include patients having an ECOG performance status of 0 or 1 and at least one measurable lesion as defined by RECIST v1.1. Key exclusion criteria included symptomatic brain metastases, evidence of interstitial lung disease or non-infectious pneumonitis and active immunodeficiency.
L702: ## Study Endpoints
L703: ## The Applicant's Description:
L704: ## Study 20200491
L705: The primary objectives of the study were to:
L706: - evaluate safety and efficacy (per RECIST 1.1 by investigator) of 2 dose levels of tarlatamab (Part 1 only)
- evaluate antitumor activity of tarlatamab as determined by ORR per RECIST 1.1 by blinded independent central review (BICR) (Parts 1 and 2)
- evaluate safety of reduced mandatory monitoring period in cycle 1 at selected dose of tarlatamab (Part 3)
L707: Secondary objectives for all parts of the study included other measures of efficacy (endpoints of DOR, DCR, PFS, and OS), safety, PK, and immunogenicity.
L708: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L709: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the endpoints in Study 20200491. FDA considers ORR an appropriate efficacy endpoint to support accelerated approval, where ORR is of sufficient magnitude and duration with a corresponding 95% confidence interval excluding clinically irrelevant response rates for the proposed dose (10 mg dose). Additionally, while the statistical analysis plan included summary statistics of PFS and OS as secondary efficacy endpoints, FDA does not consider time to-event endpoints interpretable in the context of a single arm trial for the purposes of regulatory decision-making.

L721: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L722: FDA agrees with the Applicant's descriptions of the statistical analysis plan. In addition, during the pre-BLA meeting dated September 8, 2023, FDA requested that the Applicant provide a 95% CI of ORR using the Clopper-Pearson exact method, and FDA also commented that the as-treated population, which includes all patients who received at least one dose of tarlatamab, should be the primary efficacy analysis population. In the BLA submission, the Applicant provided 95% CI and included results of a post-hoc analysis of ORR and DOR in the as-treated population. FDA and the Applicant also agreed that it would be acceptable to provide updated efficacy data in the 90-day safety update report, which were provided during the review cycle.

L745: ## 8.1.2. **Study Results – Study 20200491**
L746: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L747: ## **Data:**
L748: Audits of Study 20200491 were included as part of the independent Amgen Quality, Compliance, and Audit program performed by Amgen. The audit certificate is provided in Section 16.1.8 of Study 20200491 clinical study reports (CSRs).
L749: Amgen has been closely monitoring the evolving COVID-19 situation across the globe and has, therefore, taken proactive steps to maintain the safety of study participants and support staff, including Amgen representatives, at all clinical study centers, and to maintain study data integrity while maintaining compliance with good clinical practice (GCP). Guidelines on study conduct during the COVID-19 period were documented in the respective study protocols.
L750: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L751: Study 20200491 was conducted in accordance with the Code of Federal Regulations (CFR) governing the protection of human subjects (21 CFR part 50), Institutional Review Boards (21 CFR part 56), and the obligations of clinical investigators (21 CFR 312.50 to 312.70) in accordance with GCP. Study 20200491 was conducted under Investigational New Drug 134859.
L752: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L753: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Findings were noted from the GCP site inspections as described in Section 4.1. The Applicant conducted a 100% SDR/SDV for all clinical sites that participated in Study 20200491 which yielded updated data for efficacy and safety that did not impact FDA's interpretation of the trial results in a meaningful way. This updated data was used in labeling. Based on the information provided in this application, the conduct of Study 20200491 was consistent with the International Council for Harmonization guidelines for Good Clinical Practice, U.S. Federal regulations and requirements by other applicable regulatory authorities.
