L762: ## Patient Disposition
L763: **Study 20200491:** Overall, as of the data cutoff date of 27 June 2023, 222 subjects were randomized (Part 1) or enrolled (Parts 2 and 3) and 220 subjects received at least 1 dose of tarlatamab, including 99 subjects in the 10 mg target dose group across Parts 1 and 2, 87 subjects in the 100 mg target dose group in Part 1, and 34 subjects in the Part 3 modified safety monitoring 10 mg target dose group.
L764: As of the data cutoff date, 32.0% of subjects overall were continuing treatment with tarlatamab: 29.0% from the 10 mg group (Parts 1 and 2), 25.0% from the 100 mg group (Part 1), and 58.8% from Part 3 (10 mg with modified monitoring). Overall, 67.1% of subjects had discontinued tarlatamab: 70.0% from the 10 mg group (Parts 1 and 2), 73.9% from the 100 mg group (Part 1), and 41.2% from Part 3 (10 mg with modified monitoring). The most common reason for discontinuation of tarlatamab was disease progression (46.8%), followed by adverse event (6.8%), death (5.9%), and subject request (5.9%). As of the data cutoff date, 43.2% of subjects had discontinued the study; the most common reasons for study discontinuation were death (32.9%) and withdrawal of consent (9.0%) (Table 14-1.1.1 of Study 20200491).
L765: ## The Applicant's Position:
L766: As of the data cutoff dates for the Biologics License Application (BLA), 220 subjects were enrolled and received tarlatamab in Study 20200491 (99 at the 10 mg dose from Parts 1 and 2; 34 at the 10 mg dose with modified safety monitoring from Part 3). The primary analysis for the pivotal study was based on the BICR Full Analysis Set which included subjects who were randomized (Part 1) or enrolled (Part 2), received at least 1 dose of tarlatamab, and had 1 or more measurable lesions at baseline as assessed by BICR using RECIST 1.1. Safety analyses included subjects who received 3 1 dose of investigational product.
L767: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L768: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of patient disposition for Study 20200491. As previously discussed, although the Applicant presented data from the 10 and 100 mg doses for tarlatamab throughout this application, FDA review of efficacy focused on the proposed indicated dose (10 mg) for tarlatamab.
L769: As of the data cutoff date, the most common reason for discontinuation of tarlatamab 10 mg was disease progression (52%), followed by adverse event (7%), death (3%), and subject request (6%). In addition, 43% of subjects had discontinued the study; the most common reasons for study discontinuation were death (35%) and withdrawal of consent (7%)
L770: An information request was also sent to the Applicant to clarify the details for 8% of patients who discontinued safety follow-up with the reason "other". The Applicant clarified that the majority of patients refused the safety follow-up visit.
L771: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L772: ## Data:
L773: **Study 20200491:** As of the data cutoff date of 27 June 2023, a total of 81 subjects (36.8%) had at least 1 important protocol deviation (IPD) (Section 9.2 of Study 20200491). Overall, the most frequently reported (&gt; 10% of subjects overall) IPDs were other GCP deviations (19.5%) and missing predose assessments during treatment period (10.5%). No IPDs due to COVID-19 control measures were reported. Details for all important protocol deviations are provided by subject in Listing 16-2.2.1 of Study 20200491.
L774: ## The Applicant's Position:
L775: The above mentioned IPDs did not affect the overall results of the study.
L776: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L777: FDA reviewed the full list of protocol deviations provided by the Applicant. For the tarlatamab 10 mg dose, 32% of patients had at least one important protocol deviation. Overall, the most frequently reported (&gt; 10% of subjects overall) IPDs were other GCP deviations (20.2%) and missing predose assessments during treatment period (10.1%). Deviations listed under the "other GCP deviations" category were generally in regard to samples being collected without consent being obtained, samples being erroneously collected that were not required per protocol or late SAE reporting.
L778: There were also 2 patients who received the incorrect dose of study treatment. Review of these protocol deviations showed that 1 patient received 1 mg of tarlatamab instead of 10 mg of tarlatamab and a second patient received 10 mg of tarlatamab instead of 1 mg. Lastly, there were 4 patients who had eligibility criteria violations. Two patients received prior anticancer therapy within 30 days of the first dose of tarlatamab; one was within 28 days and the other was within 29 days. Two additional patients did not receive at least 2 prior lines of treatment (only received 1 prior line with platinum and PD-(L)1 therapy); one of these patients had a response to treatment.
L779: In addition, as noted in the summary from the Office of Scientific Inspections, 1 patient who received the tarlatamab 10 mg dose was found ineligible following inspection of (b) (4) (patient (b) (6)). The patient was noted to have a partial thrombosis of the abdominal aorta at screening which violates the exclusion criteria of having a history of arterial thrombosis within 12 months of the first dose of tarlatamab treatment. The patient had a response to tarlatamab treatment, but FDA assessed that this eligibility violation is unlikely to have had an impact on response to tarlatamab treatment as the violation was in relation to a criteria related to patient safety. The eligibility violation did not appear to have an impact on the patient's safety.
L780: From review of the above, FDA's assessment is that the important protocol deviations are

{Tarlatamab}
L782: unlikely to have impacted the study's results in a meaningful way.

L790: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L791: ## The Applicant's Position:
L792: Overall, the subjects with SCLC enrolled in Study 20200491 had baseline characteristics indicative of subjects with advanced SCLC. Thus, the patient population evaluated in the efficacy studies is considered representative of the overall population of patients with advanced SCLC and supportive of the proposed indication.
L793: Data from racial and ethnic minorities such as American Indian or Alaska Native, Black or African American, and Native Hawaiian or Other Pacific Islander are limited.
L794: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L795: The median age of patients enrolled is consistent with that of the US patient population with SCLC. A higher proportion of male patients were enrolled compared with female patients (71% vs. 28%) which does not align with the known distribution of sex for SCLC in the US. However, the ORR was similar between both groups of patients (38% for males vs. 46% for females).
L796: Patient enrollment into the efficacy population was multi-regional and global; the countries with the highest enrollment/randomization included: South Korea (30%), Spain (18%) and Japan (11%). Of the overall study population, no Black/African American patients were enrolled and only one patient was Hispanic/Latino. The distribution underrepresents the incidence of SCLC in these racial and ethnic minority groups. Despite these limitations, FDA agrees that the submitted data is supportive of the proposed indication for the US population. However, a postmarketing commitment (PMC) will be issued for analyses from ongoing or planned clinical trials enrolling a sufficient representation of U.S. racial and ethnic minority patients that is reflective of the US population of patients with SCLC to further characterize the safety and efficacy of tarlatamab in these patients.
L797: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L798: ## Data:
L799: ## **20200491**
L800: A total of 220 subjects received tarlatamab in this study with 99 subjects receiving 10 mg IV Q2W across Part 1 and Part 2 (combined), and treatment continued until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. Tumor assessments were performed every 6 weeks for the first 48 weeks and every 12 weeks thereafter. Overall across all parts of the study, subjects had a median (range) of 2 (1 to 8) prior lines of therapy, including prior PD-1 or PD-L1 inhibitor therapy (74.1%) and prior radiotherapy (79.1%) (Table 30). Time to progression after first-line platinum therapy was &lt; 90 days for 51 subjects (23.2%), 90 to &lt; 180 days for 47 subjects (21.4%), 180 days or more for 47 subjects (21.4%), and unknown for 75 subjects (34.1%). Most subjects had metastatic disease (95.5%), with no brain metastases (73.6%) or liver metastases (64.1%), and an ECOG score of 1 (72.7%). In the 10 mg group, 99 subjects (100%) received prior platinum therapy, 20.2% received prior topotecan therapy, and 69.7% received prior anti-PD-L1 therapy. Baseline characteristics were generally consistent across the dose groups.
L801: A post hoc platinum sensitivity analysis was performed for Parts 1 and 2 for progression-free interval after first-line platinum therapy, in which the interval was defined as time from end of platinum therapy in the first line to date of progression or start date of subsequent therapy, whichever came first. Of 185 subjects, progression-free interval was &lt; 90 days for 60 subjects (38 subjects at 10 mg dose), ≥ 90 days for 109 subjects (55 subjects at 10 mg dose), and unknown for 16 subjects (6 subjects at 10 mg dose) (Table 22 of Module 2.7.3, SCE).
L802: A post hoc sensitivity analysis was also performed for Parts 1 and 2 for prior lines of therapy representing global standard of care. Of 185 subjects, the number of subjects who received the following therapies at any line were 41 for topotecan, 96 for any topoisomerase 1 inhibitor (topotecan or belotecan or irinotecan), 13 for lurbinectedin, and 17 for amrubicin (Table 23 of Module 2.7.3, SCE).

L818: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L819: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's characterization of the baseline characteristics of patients who were enrolled in Study 20200491. Through information requests, the Applicant clarified that all patients who received 10 mg tarlatamab from Parts 1 and 2 of Study 20200491 had ES-SCLC, 97% of patients had metastatic disease and 73 patients (74%) had received prior anti-PD-(L)1 therapy. Of these 73 patients, 58 (59%) received anti-PD-(L)1 therapy in combination with platinum-based chemotherapy in the first line setting which is representative of frontline standard of care treatment in the US. In addition, 50 patients (51%) received prior topoisomerase-I inhibitors including 20% of patients who received prior topotecan. Of the 49 patients who did not receive a topoisomerase I inhibitor, the majority (89%) received treatments that are considered standard of care options in the US, including platinum-based chemotherapy rechallenge, lurbinectedin, CAV, temozolomide, paclitaxel. Other treatments received include amrubicin, PD-(L)1 in combination with experimental agents and gemcitabine in combination with paclitaxel. L826: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L827: ## Data:
L828: ## Treatment Compliance
L829: **Study 20200491:** When subjects were dosed at the site, they received tarlatamab directly from the investigator or designee, under medical supervision. The date and time of each dose administered were recorded in the source documents and in the electronic case report form (eCRF). Additional details on the method used to assess treatment compliance are provided in Protocol Section 6.5.
L830: ## Concomitant Medications
L831: Throughout the study, investigators could prescribe any concomitant medications or treatments deemed necessary to provide adequate supportive care except for those listed in Protocol Section 6.1.6 of Study 20200491.
L832: **Study 20200491:** Overall, concomitant medication use was reported for 217 subjects (98.6%). The concomitant medications (used in \ge 10\% of subjects) are presented in Table 12-15 of Study 20200491. Of these, the most common (\ge 20\% of subjects) were paracetamol (82.3%), dexamethasone (42.3%), sodium chloride (29.1%), magnesium oxide (25.5%), omeprazole (22.7%), lactulose (22.3%), and metoclopramide (20.0%). Concomitant CRS medication use by preferred term is provided in Table 14-8.11 of Study 20200491.
L833: ## Rescue Medication
L834: Not applicable
L835: ## The Applicant's Position:
L836: No formal treatment compliance measurements were planned. Analyses for exposure demonstrated that, the median relative dose intensity of tarlatamab was 93% and 90% for the 10 and 100 mg dose groups, respectively, in Study 20200491. Concomitant medication use was consistent with protocol-specified criteria.
L837: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L838: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of treatment compliance along with concomitant and rescue medications. In addition, pretreatment with dexamethasone 1 hour prior to administration of tarlatamab on Cycle 1 Day 1 and Day 8 and treatment with 1 liter of normal saline over 4 to 5 hours following all tarlatamab doses in Cycle 1 was required for all patients per protocol.
L839: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L840: ## Data:
L841: ## Study 20200491
L842: A total of 185 and 186 subjects were included in the BICR or Investigator Full Analysis/Safety Analysis Set, respectively. The key efficacy results for tumor response from the phase 2 Study 20200491 (safety analysis set) Part 1 and 2 are provided in Table 32.
L843: Objective response was defined as a best overall response of either complete response or partial response per RECIST 1.1. Of the 186 evaluable subjects in the Safety Analysis Set for Parts 1 and 2 (10 and 100 mg), 7 subjects (3.8%) had a confirmed complete response and 63 subjects (33.9%) had a confirmed partial response. The ORR (95% CI) by BICR was 41.4% (31.6, 51.8) for subjects who received the 10 mg target dose and 33.3% (23.6, 44.3) for subjects who received the 100 mg target dose. Within the 10 mg dose, the lower limit of the 95% CI excluded the prespecified benchmark ORR of 15%. The overall ORR (95% CI) by BICR was 37.6% (30.7, 45.0) (Table 32).
L844: Duration of response was defined as time from first evidence of complete response or partial response to disease progression or death due to any cause, whichever occurred first. For Parts 1 and 2 overall (10 and 100 mg), the median (95% CI) DOR among confirmed responders as assessed by BICR was 8.3 (6.9, not estimable [NE]) months, and the median (95% CI) follow-up time for DOR was 9.5 (8.2, 9.7) months (Table 8 of Module 2.7.3, SCE). The median (range) time to objective response was 1.4 (1.1 to 9.6) months. In the 10 mg group, the median (95% CI) DOR was not reached (5.9, NE months) (range: 2.4-12.4+ months). In the 100 mg group, it was 7.6 (5.6, NE months) (range: 1.4+ to 12.6+). The median (95% CI) follow-up time for DOR was 8.3 (7.2, 9.6) months in the 10 mg group and 9.7 (8.2, 9.9) months in the 100 mg group. The overall median time to objective response was 1.4 months in both dose groups (Table 8 of Module 2.7.3, SCE).
L845: Of the 41 responders in the 10 mg target dose group, 23 subjects (56.1%) and 10 subjects (24.4%) had a DOR of at least 6 and 9 months, respectively. As of 27 June 2023, 22 of 41 responders (53.7%) in the 10 mg target dose group were still having ongoing responses (on treatment without disease progression or death), including 18 subjects (43.9%) whose responses reached at least 6 months and still had ongoing response (Table 14-4.3.2 of Study 20200491).
L846: Of the 33 subjects in the BICR Interim RECIST Analysis Set for Part 3, 4 subjects (12.1%) had a confirmed partial response. As of the data cutoff date, 5 subjects (15.2%) had an unconfirmed partial response awaiting a confirmatory scan. The overall ORR by BICR using a 97.5% CI was 12.1% (2.8%, 30.6%) for confirmed responders and

L871: ## *Efficacy Update as of 02 October 2023*
L872: As of the data cutoff date 02 October 2023, of the 186 subjects in the Safety Analysis Set for Parts 1 and 2, 8 subjects (4.3%) had a confirmed complete response and 62 subjects (33.3%) had a confirmed partial response as assessed by BICR (Table 33.). The overall ORR (95% CI) by BICR was 37.6% (30.7%, 45.0%). The ORR (95% CI) was 41.4% (31.6%, 51.8%) for subjects who received the 10 mg target dose and 33.3% (23.6%, 44.3%) for subjects who received the 100 mg target dose (Table 33. and Figure 16). These results are consistent with those from the primary analysis (Table 32).
L873: In Parts 1 and 2 as assessed by investigator, no subject had a confirmed complete response and 70 subjects (37.6%) had a confirmed partial response. The ORR (95% CI) was 41.4% (31.6%, 51.8%) for subjects who received the 10 mg target dose and 33.3% (23.6%, 44.3%) for subjects who received the 100 mg target dose. The overall ORR (95% CI) by investigator assessment was 37.6% (30.7%, 45.0%) (90-day Table 14-4.1.1 of Study 20200491 and 90-day Figure 14-4.1.1 of Study 20200491).
L874: Analyses were conducted for best overall response to evaluate concordance between assessments by BICR (primary endpoint) and the study center investigator (secondary endpoint). The concordance rate between BICR and investigator assessment for responder (complete response or partial response) vs nonresponder was 89.0% (90-day Table 14-4.2 of Study 20200491).
L875: For Parts 1 and 2 overall, the median (95% CI) DOR among confirmed responders as assessed by BICR was 9.7 (6.9, NE) months (8.3 [6.9, NE] months at primary analysis). In the 10 mg target dose group (Parts 1 and 2), the response appears rapid and durable with median (range) time to response (TTR) of 1.4 (1.1 to 2.8) months and a median (95% CI) DOR of 9.7 (5.9, NE) months. Of the 41 responders in the 10 mg target dose group, 27 subjects (65.9%) and 17 subjects (41.5%) had a DOR of at least 6 and 9 months, respectively; 11 subjects (26.8%) had a DOR of at least 12 months, all increased since primary analysis. As of 02 October 2023, 19 of 41 responders (46.3%) in the 10 mg target dose group were still having ongoing responses (on treatment without disease progression or death), including 15 subjects (36.6%) whose responses reached at least 9 months and were still ongoing (an increase from 10 subjects at primary analysis) (90-day Table 14-4.3.2 of Study 20200491).

L895: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L896: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L897: The observed ORR in the pivotal study, which was rapid and durable, represents a clinically meaningful benefit when considering the intended patient population with life-threatening disease and limited available therapies (Section 2).
L898: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L899: FDA considers the ORR endpoint by BICR as the primary endpoint, while DoR is a supportive secondary endpoint. Note that analysis of DCR and proportion of tumor shrinkage is generally considered exploratory since the DCR is not a well-established measure of clinical benefit.
L900: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's summary of the efficacy results above since the results were updated from re-adjudication of previous target lesion assessments and none of the 25 previously unsubmitted scans submitted for BICR factored into any update of response (see Section 4.1 for details). In the updated data submitted to FDA on March 15, 2024, by the Applicant, the best overall response of patient [REDACTED](b) (6) was changed from stable disease to partial response, and the ORR by BICR was consequently updated from 40% to 41%. In the FDA's analyses, the number of responders by BICR in the 10 mg target dose group in Parts 1 and 2 remains 40, and the ORR by BICR was 40% (95% CI: 31%, 51%). Among the 40 responders in the 10 mg target dose group in Parts 1 and 2, the median DoR was not reached at the data cutoff date of October 2, 2023 (90-day update). To better characterize the DoR, the data was summarized with additional follow-up time, as requested by FDA. At the latest data cutoff date of January 12, 2024, the median DoR was 9.7 months (95% CI: 6.9, NE and range: 2.7, 20.7+); and 68% and 40% of the responders had an observed DoR \ge6 months and \ge12 months, respectively. The median follow-up time for DoR was 15.1 months (estimated using the reverse Kaplan-Meier Method). See Table 34 for the FDA's summary of the efficacy results.

L920: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L921: ## Data:
L922: ## Study 20200491
L923: The PFS and OS results provide additional evidence of the efficacy of tarlatamab. In Study 20200491 Parts 1 and 2 overall, the median (95% CI) PFS by BICR was 4.0 (2.9, 5.4) months, with a median follow-up time of 9.7 months, and Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS of 36.1% at 6 months and 23.4% at 12 months (Table 14-4.5.2 of Study 20200491 and Section 2.1.4.4 of Module 2.7.3, SCE). Results for the 10 and 100 mg dose groups (Parts 1 and 2) were comparable: in the 10 mg dose group, the median (95% CI) PFS by BICR was 4.3 (3.0, 5.6) months, with a median follow-up time of 9.7 months, and Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS of 39.7% at 6 months and 25.7% at 12 months; in the 100 mg dose group, the median (95% CI) PFS by BICR was 3.2 (2.6, 4.2) months, with a median follow-up time of 10.8 months, and Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS of 31.9% at 6 months and 20.7% at 12 months.
L924: Median (range) follow-up time for OS for Parts 1 and 2 overall was 10.6 (9.7 to 11.3) months, and the median (95% CI) OS was not reached (12.4 to NE months); the Kaplan-Meier estimate for OS at 6 months was 72.5% and 59.8% at 12 months, with similar results for the 10 and 100 mg dose groups (Table 14-4.6.1 of Study 20200491 and Section 2.1.4.5 of Module 2.7.3, SCE). As of the data cutoff date, 101 of the 186 subjects (54.3%) were alive at last follow-up visit, 19 (10.2%) had withdrawn consent, 1 (0.5%) was lost to follow-up, and 65 (34.9%) had died (Table 14-4.6.1 of Study 20200491).

L942: ## *Efficacy Update as of 02 October 2023*
L943: In Study 20200491 Parts 1 and 2 overall, the Kaplan-Meier estimate of median (95% CI) PFS by BICR was 4.0 (2.9, 5.4) months (90-day Table 14-4.5.2 of Study 20200491). In the 10 mg dose group (Parts 1 and 2), median (95% CI) PFS based on BICR was 4.3 (3.0, 5.6) months, with a median follow-up time of 13.6 months (90-day Table 14-4.5.2 of Study 20200491 and Figure 19). The Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS at 6 and 12 months were 39.7% and 25.2%, respectively. In the 100 mg target dose group, median (95% CI) PFS based on BICR was 3.2 (2.6, 4.6) months, with a median follow-up time of 11.8 months (90-day Table 14-4.5.2 of Study 20200491 and Figure 19). The Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS at 6 and 12 months were 32.8% and 19.7%, respectively. Except for a longer median follow-up time, these results are similar to those from the primary analysis (Table 14-4.6.1 of Study 20200491).
L944: For Parts 1 and 2 overall, median (range) follow-up time for OS was 13.6 (12.9, 14.3) months, and the median (95% CI) OS was 15.2 (12.3, NE) months (previously not reached at primary analysis). In the 10 mg dose group (Parts 1 and 2), median (95% CI) OS was 15.2 (10.8, NE) months, with a median follow-up time of 13.8 months (90-day Table 14-4.6.1 of Study 20200491 and Figure 20). In comparison, at the primary analysis, the median OS was lower (14.3 months) and the median follow-up time shorter (10.6 months). The Kaplan-Meier estimates for OS at 6 and 12 months were 73.4% and 56.7%, respectively. In the 100 mg dose group, median (95% CI) OS was 15.1 (10.1, NE) months (previously not reached), with a median follow-up time of 13.3 months (previously 10.6 months) (90-day Table 14-4.6.1 of Study 20200491 and Figure 20). The Kaplan-Meier estimates for OS at 6 and 12 months were 71.4% and 61.8%, respectively.
L945: As of data cutoff date 02 October 2023, 48 subjects (48.5%) and 38 subjects (43.7%) in the 10 and 100 mg groups, respectively, were alive at last follow-up (90-day Table 14-4.6.1 of Study 20200491). This number is decreased from the number of subjects alive at the primary analysis (57 and 44 in the 10 and 100 mg groups, respectively). Except for a longer median follow-up time and fewer subjects alive at last follow-up, these results are similar to those from the primary analysis.

L965: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L966: ## The Applicant's Position:
L967: The PFS and OS results in the pivotal study provides additional evidence of the efficacy of tarlatamab. No notable differences were observed among various subgroups for ORR, DOR, DoDc, PFS, or OS. However, subgroup analysis interpretation may be limited because of the small sample size of each subgroup.
L968: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L969: DoR was also a secondary endpoint for the study; FDA's assessment of the DoR endpoint is summarized above along with assessment of data from the ORR endpoint. FDA reviewed the summary of the PFS and OS data above. However, FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position regarding the interpretation of PFS and OS results as evidence for treatment effect. These endpoints are difficult to interpret in the absence of an appropriate control arm.
L970: The interpretation of the comparison of the PFS and OS results between the 10 mg dose group and 100 mg dose group in the BICR Full Analysis Set and Safety Analysis Set are also limited, as the two groups of patients were not fully randomized (the 10 mg group included patients in Part 2 who enrolled after dose selection and thus were not randomized).
L971: ## **Dose/Dose Response**
L972: See Section 6.3.2, Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L973: ## The Applicant's Position:
L974: The proposed dose of 10 mg Q2W is effective and generally well tolerated in subjects with advanced SCLC. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the observed efficacy and safety data and demonstrates a favorable benefit-risk profile. Overall, results of exposure-response analysis for efficacy measures suggest the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W exhibited near maximal efficacy in the intended patient population, and exposures associated with lower doses are estimated to have lower efficacy. None of the covariates evaluated in the exposure-response analysis resulted in clinically meaningful changes in efficacy and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.
L975: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L976: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that E-R analyses, in addition to the clinical findings from Studies 20200491 and 20160323, support the proposed 1/10 mg dosing regimen. Please see Section 6.2.2.1 for FDA's full assessment on the proposed tarlatamab dose.

The overall ORR (95% CI) by BICR was 37.6% (30.7, 45.0). Results for the efficacy update as of 02 October 2023 were comparable to those at primary analysis.

L993: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints**
L994: ## Data:
L995: An exploratory objective of the phase 2 Study 20200491 was to investigate the subject experience with tarlatamab treatment using patient-reported outcome instruments. Although exploratory, a trend toward improvement or stabilization was observed in the severity of key lung cancer symptoms of cough, dyspnea, and chest pain across both doses (10 and 100 mg in Part 1 and Part 2) when compared with baseline (Section 10.4 and Section 16.1.13.8 of Study 20200491). A consistent trend toward improvement was also observed for global health status. Most subjects reported no or little degree of bother due to treatment-related side effects associated with tarlatamab.
L996: ## The Applicant's Position:
L997: Although exploratory, subjects overall showed a trend toward improvement or stabilization was observed in the severity of key lung cancer symptoms of cough, dyspnea, and chest pain when compared with baseline.
L998: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}

L1000: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1001: FDA reviewed the summary of the PRO data above. However, treatment effect on these endpoints is difficult to interpret in the absence of an appropriate control arm.

L1009: ## 8.1.3. **Supportive Study 20160323**
L1010: ## **Trial Design**
L1011: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1012: **Study 20160323** is an ongoing, phase 1, FIH, open-label, ascending, multiple-dose study evaluating tarlatamab as monotherapy and in combination with anti-PD-1 therapy (Figure 21). Tarlatamab is administered as an IV infusion Q2W or Q3W (in Part G) (with or without step dosing) in subjects with advanced SCLC that progressed or recurred after at least 1 platinum-based regimen. The study consists of multiple parts designed to evaluate the safety and tolerability of tarlatamab alone (Parts A, D, E, and G) or in combination with pembrolizumab (Part C) and to determine the maximum tolerated dose or recommended phase 2 dose of tarlatamab alone (Part A1) or in combination with pembrolizumab (Part C). Secondary objectives were to characterize the PK and evaluate preliminary antitumor activity of tarlatamab alone or in combination with pembrolizumab (Figure 21). See Section 8.1 of Study 20160323 for full details of the study design.
L1013: Figure 21. Applicant – Schema of Study 20160323
L1014: CRS = Cytokine Release Syndrome; ES = extensive stage; IV = intravenous; MTD = maximum tolerated dose; Q2W = every 2 weeks; RP2D = recommended phase 2 dose; R/R = relapsed/refractory; SCLC = small cell lung cancer; US = United States
L1015: Tarlatamab was administered as a short-term IV dose Q2W beginning in cycle 2. In cycle 1, step dosing may be implemented.
L1016: a Depending on the observed safety data, a second cohort will be enrolled to test an increased dose.
L1017: b Tarlatamab and pembrolizumab was administered using a staggered dosing schedule, with tarlatamab administered Q2W starting on day 1, and pembrolizumab administered Q3W starting on day 15.
L1018: c Part B will not be opened.
L1019: d Among the 30 subjects in Part F, 10 subjects from US sites only may be enrolled in the wearable digital device substudy.
L1020: e Among the 32 subjects in Part G, 10 subjects from US sites only may be enrolled in the wearable digital device substudy.
L1021: f Enrollment has been completed.
L1022: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
{Tarlatamab}
L1023: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1024: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study design.
L1025: ## Eligibility Criteria
L1026: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1027: ## **Study 20160323**
L1028: Eligible subjects were 318 years of age with relapsed or refractory SCLC who progressed or recurred following at least 1 platinum-based regimen. Subjects must have had measurable disease per modified Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST) version 1.1, an ECOG performance status of 0 to 2, and adequate organ function. Eligibility criteria included adequate cardiac, pulmonary, kidney, and liver function. Subjects with acute and/or uncontrolled active systemic infection, active hepatitis B or C, or human immunodeficiency virus infection or COVID-19 infection were excluded.
L1029: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1030: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the eligibility criteria.
L1031: ## Study Endpoints
L1032: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1033: **Study 20160323:** The primary objectives of the study were to evaluate the safety and tolerability of tarlatamab and to determine the maximum tolerated dose or recommended phase 2 dose of tarlatamab. Secondary objectives included characterizing the PK of tarlatamab and evaluating preliminary antitumor activity of tarlatamab.
L1034: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1035: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study endpoints.
L1036: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L1037: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1038: **Study 20160323:** There were 3 amendments to the original SAP before the data cutoff date of 28 March 2023 for this study as summarized in Table 8-8 of Study 20160323. Full statistical methods for this supportive study are provided in Section 8.8 of Study 20160323.
L1039: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1040: FDA agrees with the Applicant's above descriptions of the statistical analysis plan and amendments.
L1041: ## 8.1.4. **Study Results – Study 20160323**
L1042: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L1043: ## **Data:**
L1044: Audits of Study 20160323 were included as part of the independent Amgen Quality, Compliance, and Audit program performed by Amgen. The audit certificates are provided in Section 16.1.8 of Study 20160323 clinical study reports.
L1045: Amgen has been closely monitoring the evolving COVID-19 situation across the globe and has, therefore, taken proactive steps to maintain the safety of study participants and support staff, including Amgen representatives, at all clinical study centers, and to maintain study data integrity while maintaining compliance with GCP. Guidelines on study conduct during the COVID-19 period were documented in the respective study protocols.
L1046: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1047: Study 20160323 was conducted in accordance with the CFR governing the protection of human subjects (21 CFR part 50), Institutional Review Boards (21 CFR part 56), and the obligations of clinical investigators (21 CFR 312.50 to 312.70) in accordance with GCP. Study 20160323 was conducted under Investigational New Drug 134859.
L1048: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1049: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L1057: ## Patient Disposition
L1058: **Study 20160323:** Overall, as of the data cutoff date of 28 March 2023, 208 subjects were enrolled in Study 20160323, and 205 subjects received at least 1 dose of tarlatamab, including 27 subjects in the 10 mg Q2W dose group, 63 subjects in the 100 mg Q2W dose group, and 13 subjects in the 100 mg Q2W dose group with modified safety monitoring. As of the data
L1059: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L1060: cutoff date, 180 subjects (86.5%) discontinued tarlatamab. The most frequent reason for discontinuation was disease progression (68.8%) (Table 14-1.1 of Study 20160323).
L1061: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1062: As of the data cutoff dates for the Biologics License Application (BLA), 205 subjects were enrolled and received tarlatamab in Study 20160323 (27 at the 10 mg dose).
L1063: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1064: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of patient disposition. The focus of FDA's review for this study will be based upon the patients who received tarlatamab at the proposed indicated dose (10 mg dose).
L1065: ## **Protocol Violations/Deviations**
L1066: ## Data:
L1067: **Study 20160323:** As of the data cutoff date 28 March 2023, a total of 158 subjects (77.1%) had at least 1 IPD. Important protocol deviations were consistent across groups, with the exception of subjects in the 10 mg monotherapy dose group who had a more than 10% higher rate of IPDs (88.9%) than the overall population and subjects in the 100 mg dose with 24-hour monitoring group who had a more than 10% lower rate of IPDs (53.8%) than the overall population (Section 9.2 of Study 20160323). Overall, the most frequently reported (&gt; 10% of subjects) IPDs were missing data (65.9%) and received the wrong treatment or incorrect dose (10.2%).
L1068: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1069: The above mentioned IPDs did not affect the overall results of the study.
L1070: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1071: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. From the 27 patients who received the tarlatamab 10 mg dose, 24 patients (89%) reported at least one IPD. The most frequently reported (&gt;10% of patients) IPDs were missing data 70.4% and receiving the wrong treatment or incorrect dose (11.4%) in the 10 mg dose group.
L1072: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L1073: ## Data:
L1074: **Study 20160323:** In the overall population (N= 205), more than half of subjects (56.1%) were men, mostly White (78.0%), and not Hispanic or Latino (77.6%). The median (range) age was 63 (32 to 80) years, with more than half of all subjects younger than 65 years (60.5%) (Table 35).

L1094: ## Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L1095: ## Data:
L1096: ## **Study 20160323**
L1097: Overall, subjects had a median (range) of 2.0 (1 to 7) prior lines of therapy, including prior PD-1 or PD-L1 inhibitor therapy (61.0%). Most subjects had received prior radiotherapy for current malignancy (77.1%). Most subjects had metastatic disease (92.2%) and an ECOG performance score of 1 (61.5%). Prior to tarlatamab treatment, subjects' response to their most recent platinum-based anticancer treatment was as follows:
L1098: - 47.3% of subjects were sensitive to the most recent platinum-based therapy, defined as best response of stable disease or better and an interval of 3 90 days between the date of the last dose of platinum in the most recent platinum-containing prior line and the start date.
- 28.3% of subjects were refractory, defined as a best response of progressive disease to most recent line of platinum-based chemotherapy.
- 20.0% of subjects were resistant, defined as a best response of stable disease or better and a treatment-free interval of &lt; 90 days between the date of most recent line of platinum-based chemotherapy and the start date of the next line of treatment.
L1099: Baseline characteristics for Study 20160323 are provided in Table 13 of Module 2.7.3, SCE.
L1100: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1101: Overall, the subjects with SCLC enrolled across phase 1 and 2 studies had baseline characteristics indicative of subjects with advanced SCLC. Thus, the patient population evaluated in the efficacy studies is considered representative of the overall population of patients with advanced SCLC and supportive of the proposed indication.
L1102: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1103: FDA reviewed the results presented above for the other baseline characteristics but did not verify the results since this study was supportive and the results were not included in labeling.
L1104: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L1105: ## Data:
L1106: ## **Treatment Compliance**
L1107: **Study 20160323:** Investigational products were administered at the study center by a qualified staff member. A physician must have been available at the time of administration of investigational products (Protocol Section 6.2). The date, time, package lot number, dose, and the start and stop time of infusion were recorded (Protocol Section 6.2.5).
L1108: ## Concomitant Medications
L1109: Throughout the study, investigators could prescribe any concomitant medications or treatments deemed necessary to provide adequate supportive care except for those listed in Protocol Section 6.9 of Study 20160323.
L1110: Overall, concomitant medication use was reported for 204 of 205 subjects (99.5%). The concomitant medication categories with the highest incidence rates (&gt; 10% of subjects) are presented in Table 12-14 of Study 20160323. Overall, the most frequently reported concomitant medications (\ge 25% of subjects) were paracetamol (85.9%), dexamethasone (82.4%), sodium chloride (56.6%), ondansetron (32.2%), and omeprazole (28.3%).
L1111: ## Rescue Medication
L1112: Not applicable
L1113: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1114: No formal treatment compliance measurements were planned. Analyses for exposure demonstrated that regardless of dose, the median relative dose intensity of tarlatamab was 100% in Study 20160323. Concomitant medication use was consistent with protocol-specified criteria.
L1115: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1116: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of compliance, concomitant and rescue medication.
L1117: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L1118: ## Data:
L1119: ## **Study 20160323**
L1120: At the 10 mg monotherapy target dose (N = 24 evaluable subjects in the Interim Efficacy Analysis Set), 8 subjects had a confirmed response, including 2 subjects (8.3%) with a complete response and 6 subjects (25.0%) with a partial response. The confirmed ORR (95% CI) based on investigator assessment was 33.3% (15.6%, 55.3%). In addition, 1 subject had an unconfirmed partial response awaiting confirmatory scan for a confirmed and unconfirmed ORR (95% CI) of 37.5% (18.8%, 59.4%). The response was rapid and durable, with a median (range) time to response of 1.7 (1.6 to 11.3) months and a median (95% CI) DOR of 14.9 (3.0, NE) months. The median (95% CI) follow-up time for DOR was 18.8 (1.0, NE) months.
L1121: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L1122: At the 100 mg monotherapy target dose (N = 76 evaluable subjects in the Interim Efficacy Analysis Set), 15 subjects had a confirmed response, including 2 subjects (2.6%) with a complete response and 13 subjects (17.1%) with a partial response. The ORR (95% CI) based on investigator assessment was 19.7% (11.5%, 30.5%). In addition, 2 subjects had unconfirmed partial responses awaiting confirmatory scan for a confirmed and unconfirmed ORR (95% CI) of 22.4% (13.6%, 33.4%). The median (range) time to response was 1.8 (1.6 to 3.7) months; the median (95% CI) DOR was not reached (5.6, NE) months. The median (95% CI) follow-up time for DOR was 18.7 (12.8, 23.8) months.
L1123: ## *Efficacy Update as of 02 October 2023*
L1124: As of the 02 October 2023 data cutoff date, tarlatamab monotherapy and combination therapy for SCLC subjects at multiple doses resulted in confirmed ORR (95% CI) of 22.2% (16.9%, 28.2%), including 3 additional subjects with confirmed responses since the original cutoff date (49 subjects [02 October 2023] vs 46 subjects [28 March 2023]). One additional subject had an unconfirmed partial response and was awaiting confirmatory scan. The ORR (95% CI) for the 49 subjects with confirmed response and 1 subject with unconfirmed partial response awaiting confirmatory scan was 22.6% (17.3%, 28.7%). The DCR (95% CI) was 52.5% (45.7%, 59.2%) (90-day Table 14-4.1.1 of Study 20160323). These updated results are consistent with the results presented in the primary analysis (Section 2.2.3 of Module 2.7.3, Summary of Clinical Efficacy).
L1125: The median DOR (95% CI) was 11.2 (6.6, 14.9) months for the 49 confirmed responders (90-day Table 14-4.2.1 of Study 20160323).
L1126: Updated Kaplan-Meier plot for DOR is provided in 90-day Figure 14-4.3.1. An updated waterfall plot of best overall response is provided in 90-day Figure 14-4.1 of Study 20160323. An updated swimmer plot of time to response and duration of treatment among confirmed responders in the Interim Analysis Efficacy Set is provided in 90-day Figure 14-4.2 of Study 20160323.
L1127: These results are consistent with the interim analyses presented in the initial marketing application (Section 2.2.3 of Module 2.7.3, Summary of Clinical Efficacy), and this updated efficacy information aligns with the overall study conclusions as originally presented (Study 20160323 Interim Analysis CSR).
L1128: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1129: These efficacy results from phase 1 were supportive of those observed in the phase 2 study. The FIH efficacy results across all dose cohorts are summarized in Section 2.2.3 of Module 2.7.3, SCE; full results are provided in the Study 20160323 clinical study report.

L1142: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1143: ## Data:
L1144: ## **Study 20160323**
L1145: In the 10 mg target dose group, median (95% CI) PFS based on investigator review was 3.5 (1.9, 11.0) months, with a median follow-up time of 20.5 months. The Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS at 6 and 12 months were 36.3% and 24.2%, respectively. Median (95% CI) OS was 20.3 (5.1, NE) months, with a median follow-up time of 12.6 months. The Kaplan-Meier estimates for OS at 6 and 12 months were 61.8% and 54.9%, respectively.
L1146: In the 100 mg target dose group, median (95% CI) PFS based on investigator review was 3.7 (1.9, 4.5) months, with a median follow-up time of 18.8 months. The Kaplan-Meier estimates for PFS at 6 and 12 months were 32.1% and 18.6%, respectively. Median (95% CI) OS was not reached (11.0, NE) months, with a median follow-up time of 12.0 months. The Kaplan-Meier estimates for OS at 6 and 12 months were 79.3% and 60.4%, respectively.
L1147: ## Efficacy Update as of 02 October 2023
L1148: The Kaplan-Meier estimate of median (95% CI) PFS of all subjects was 3.3 (2.0, 3.7) months (90-day Table 14-4.3.1 of Study 20160323). At the time of the data cutoff, the Kaplan-Meier estimate of median (95% CI) OS was 12.8 (10.9, 20.3) months (90-day Table 14-4.4.1 of Study 20160323). These results are consistent with the interim analyses presented in the initial marketing application (Section 2.2.3 of Module 2.7.3, Summary of Clinical Efficacy), and this updated efficacy information aligns with the overall study conclusions as originally presented (Study 20160323 Interim Analysis CSR).
L1149: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1150: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
L1151: {Tarlatamab}
L1152: The PFS and OS results in the phase 1 study provide additional evidence of the efficacy of tarlatamab.
L1153: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1154: FDA reviewed the efficacy results presented above but did not verify the results since this study was supportive and the results were not included in labeling. FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position on PFS and OS results since these time-to-event endpoints are difficult to interpret in the absence of an appropriate control arm.

L1162: ## **8.1.5. Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1163: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1164: Efficacy data from Parts 1 and 2 of Study 20200491 in patients who received the tarlatamab 10 mg proposed dose comprise the primary dataset to support this application. See Section 8.1.2 for detailed discussion of these results. Efficacy data from Part 3 of Study 20200491 and in patients who received the tarlatamab 10 mg dose in Study 20160323 were evaluated as supportive evidence of effectiveness for this application.
L1165: The proposed indication by the Applicant (SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy) differs from the population studied in the 20200491 study (SCLC with disease progression after receiving previous treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of prior therapy). To evaluate the appropriateness of the Applicant's proposal, FDA performed analyses of efficacy subgroups by prior therapies from Study 20200491 which are further described in Section 8.1.7.
L1166: ## 8.1.6. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1167: ## Primary Endpoints
L1168: ## Data:
L1169: See Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses).
L1170: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1171: The primary evidence for efficacy for this marketing application is the evaluation of the data for 222 subjects with advanced SCLC who received tarlatamab monotherapy (10 or 100 mg) in the pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491, as reflected in a durable objective response in a large percentage of subjects and supported by the totality of clinically significant response measures.
L1172: The efficacy results from the 208 subjects enrolled in FIH Study 20160323 provided supportive efficacy data.
L1173: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1174: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position, with the exception that this review did not rely on efficacy data from patients treated at the 100 mg dose. As described in Section 8.1.5, the primary evidence for efficacy for this marketing application is from the evaluation of data from 99 patients with ES-SCLC who received tarlatamab 10 mg from Parts 1 and 2 of Study 20200491. The efficacy results from Part 3 of Study 20200491, along with those from the 24 efficacy evaluable patients who received the tarlatamab 10 mg dose in Study 20160323 provide supportive efficacy data.
L1175: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L1176: ## Data:
L1177: See Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints.
L1178: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1179: Overall, efficacy data for the phase 1 study (20160323) are consistent with the phase 2 efficacy results (from Study 20200491) in subjects with advanced SCLC treated with tarlatamab 10 mg monotherapy.
L1180: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1181: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. The ORR results from the 24 efficacy
L1182: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
L1183: {Tarlatamab}
L1184: evaluable patients from Study 20160323 are generally consistent with the Study 20200491 efficacy results.
L1185: Additionally, in Study 20200491, 34 patients received tarlatamab at the proposed dosing regimen in Part 3 of the study, which was primarily intended to evaluate the safety of tarlatamab under reduced monitoring criteria for CRS. The confirmed BICR ORR per RECIST v1.1 was 32% (95% CI: 17%, 51%). The DoR estimate is premature as 73% of the patients who had responses were censored for the DoR analysis. These ORR results are generally consistent with the results observed in Parts 1 and 2 (40% vs. 32%).
L1186: ## Subpopulations
L1187: Analyses for ORR, DOR, DoDC, PFS, and OS in Study 20200491 Parts 1 and 2 (10 and 100 mg) were conducted to explore the consistency of tarlatamab treatment effect across subgroups of age, race, region, number of prior lines of anticancer therapy, prior PD-1 or PD-L1 therapy, sum of diameters of target lesions at baseline, platinum sensitivity, presence of brain or liver metastasis at baseline, and DLL3 cut points. No notable differences were observed among these subgroups for ORR (Figures 7 and 8 of Module 2.5, Clinical Overview), DOR, DoDC, PFS, or OS. However, subgroup analysis interpretation may be limited because of the small sample size of each subgroup.
L1188: A post hoc analysis performed for Parts 1 and 2 (10 and 100 mg) of the progression-free interval after first-line platinum therapy, which included time to next therapy for subjects with unknown progression dates after first-line platinum therapy, showed that efficacy was observed across all subgroups. Objective response rate, DOR, DoDC, PFS, and OS across these subgroups by progression-free interval after first-line platinum treatment were generally consistent with the primary analyses (Table 4 of Module 2.5 [Clinical Overview] for ORR and DOR).
L1189: A post hoc analysis performed for Parts 1 and 2 (10 and 100 mg) of prior lines of therapy showed that efficacy was observed across subgroups of subjects who had (or had not) received a variety of prior lines of therapy representing global standard of care, including topotecan, lurbinectedin, and amrubicin. Objective response rate, DOR, PFS, and OS across these subgroups by prior line of therapy were generally consistent with the primary analyses (Table 5 of Module 2.5 [Clinical Overview] for ORR and DOR).
L1190: Analyses were conducted for best overall response to evaluate concordance between assessments by BICR (primary endpoint) and the study center investigator (secondary endpoint). Of the 186 subjects, 164 subjects had scans evaluable by BICR and the investigator; of these 164 subjects, 146 subjects (89.0%) were concordant (Table 14-4.2 of Study 20200491).
L1191: A post hoc subgroup analysis by sex and ECOG performance status at baseline also showed that efficacy was observed across these subgroups; ORR, DOR, DoDC, PFS, and OS across these subgroups were generally consistent with those from the primary analyses.
L1192: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1193: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
L1194: ## {Tarlatamab}
L1195: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Forest plots of the subgroup analyses for Part 1 and 2 (10 mg only) are presented in Figure 22. Review showed generally consistent ORRs across multiple subgroups including age, sex, race, number of prior lines of anticancer therapy, prior PD-(L)1 therapy, and DLL3 cutpoints. DLL3 cutpoints were previously validated by the Applicant at the 25% and 75% cutoffs at moderate (2+) and strong (3+) staining intensity. Review of efficacy data at these cutoffs did not show any differences in ORR across subgroups. There were also 2 patients who were also DLL3 negative, with 1 patient responding to tarlatamab treatment.
L1196: **Figure 22. Forest Plot of Subgroup Analyses of Objective Response Rate at 10 mg Tarlatamab Dose as Assessed by BICR (BICR Full Analysis Set for Part 1 and Part 2) (Study 20200491 Primary Analysis).**
L1197: Data cutoff date: 27 June 2023.
L1198: Source: Figure 7 of Module 2.5 in the Applicant's Clinical Overview.
L1199: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L1200: Although based on its mechanism of action a differential effect is not expected for tarlatamab in patients with platinum-sensitive vs platinum-resistant disease, data was also reviewed regarding patient's sensitivity to platinum. Of the 99 patients who received tarlatamab 10 mg, data on platinum sensitivity was available in 69 patients. The ORR was 52% (95% CI: 32%, 71%) in 27 patients with platinum-resistant SCLC (defined as &lt; 90 days after end of first line platinum therapy to date of progression) and 31% (95% CI: 18%, 47%) in 42 patients with platinum-sensitive SCLC (defined as ≥90 days after end of first line platinum therapy to date of progression). The ORR for the 30 patients with data unavailable for platinum sensitivity was 43% (95% CI: 25%, 63%). Although the ORR point estimates differ between these subgroups, the 95% CI overlap, and given the known mechanism of action of tarlatamab as a targeted agent that causes activation of the immune system, FDA considers that a true difference in efficacy based on platinum sensitivity status is unlikely with tarlatamab.
L1201: Supportive analysis was performed by patient's sensitivity to platinum using an alternative definition where the patient's progression free interval was defined as time from first line platinum treatment to disease progression or start of second line treatment. Using this alternative definition, the ORR was 45% (95% CI: 29%, 62%) in 38 patients with platinum-sensitive disease and 38% (95% CI: 25%, 52%) in 55 patients with platinum-resistant disease with a 50% ORR in 6 patients with unknown platinum sensitivity. This analysis is encouraging to suggest that there is no difference to tarlatamab response by platinum sensitivity status. Further information regarding this will be available from the confirmatory trial.
L1202: For this application the proposed indication from the Applicant (SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy) differs from the population enrolled in Study 20200491 (SCLC with disease progression after receiving previous treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of prior therapy). FDA conducted multiple subgroup analyses based on prior therapies in Parts 1 and 2 of Study 20200491 in patients who received the 10 mg dose. The ORR in patients who received 2 lines of prior therapy was 42% (95% CI: 29%, 54%) in 65 patients, and ORR was 38% (95% CI: 21%, 56%) in 32 patients who received 3 or more lines of prior therapy. Of these patients, 50 received a topoisomerase I inhibitor (topotecan, irinotecan or belotecan) as a prior treatment while 47 did not. The ORR in patients who received a prior topoisomerase I inhibitor was 46% (95% CI: 32%, 61%), while the ORR in patients who did not receive a prior topoisomerase I inhibitor was 41% (95% CI: 27%, 56%). Given that topoisomerase I inhibitors (including topotecan) are considered standard treatments for ES-SCLC with progression after platinum-based chemotherapy and topotecan is the only therapy with traditional approval from FDA in this setting, FDA analyzed the prior treatments received and the response rates in patients who did not receive a topoisomerase-I inhibitor per the table below.
L1203: **Table 36 Tarlatamab efficacy for second line therapies for patients who did not receive a prior topoisomerase-I inhibitor**
L1204: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L1205: Therapy	N
(n=47)	ORR
Amrubicin	5	4/5 (80%)
CAV	10	3/10 (30%)

L1206: The grand majority (42/47; 89%) of patients received standard treatment as the following are considered either preferred or other recommended regimens in the US: CAV, gemcitabine plus paclitaxel, lurbinectedin, paclitaxel, anti-PD-(L)1 antibody, temozolomide, and platinum retreatment with or without anti-PD-(L)1 therapy. The ORR is 36% in these 42 patients. Although amrubicin is not approved in the US, clinical trials have shown comparable efficacy between amrubicin and topotecan (von Pawel, 2014). In addition, upon excluding patients with either unknown platinum sensitivity or platinum resistant disease who received platinum +/- PD-L1 rechallenge (n=3 and n=3, respectively) and patients who received amrubicin (n=5), the ORR is 31% in the remaining 36 patients.
L1207: From this analysis, the majority of patients received second line treatments that are consistent with standard of care in the United States. Analysis of the ORR in patients who received prior topoisomerase I inhibitor, as well as in patients who did not receive prior topoisomerase I inhibitor (including and not including patients who received other standard of care therapies) were generally consistent with the response rate observed for the overall study population. Furthermore, it is not expected that treatment with additional therapies following platinum-based chemotherapy would increase the likelihood of response to tarlatamab relative to treatment with tarlatamab used as second-line therapy following platinum-based chemotherapy. In light of the observed results and particularly considering the ORR observed with tarlatamab in patients with platinum-resistant SCLC, which far exceeds ORR reported with standard of care therapies, the available data for tarlatamab supports the proposed indication for treatment of SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L1208: Lastly, FDA notes that subgroup analyses for time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS, and PRO endpoints, are difficult to interpret in the absence of an appropriate control arm.

L1214: ## **8.1.7. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness**
L1215: ## Data:
L1216: Data are presented above in Section 8.1.1. Efficacy data were not pooled across the phase 1 and 2 studies due to differences in study populations, number of prior cancer treatments, tarlatamab dose, or other aspects of treatment (monotherapy vs combination therapy).
L1217: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1218: Results from both Study 20200491 (phase 2) and Study 20160323 (phase 1) demonstrated the clinical activity of tarlatamab in subjects with advanced SCLC, a disease state with high unmet medical need. Both studies showed rapid, durable responses, and consistent median PFS and OS; improvements in PRO assessments were also seen with tarlatamab treatment in the phase 2 study.
L1219: Overall, tarlatamab demonstrated a clinically meaningful durable objective response and potential survival benefits of PFS and OS. Median OS and median PFS are considered potential survival benefits and will be further evaluated in the ongoing phase 3 randomized, active-controlled Study 20210004.
L1220: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1221: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that tarlatamab demonstrated clinically meaningful durable objective response. Time-to-event endpoints such as PFS and OS, and PRO endpoints are difficult to interpret in the absence of an appropriate control arm.
L1222: FDA considers the observed 40% response rate (95% CI: 31%, 51%) from patients enrolled to Parts 1 and 2 of Study 20200491 who received tarlatamab at the proposed dosing regimen a clinically meaningful improvement over available therapy. The response rates were supported by a median DoR of 9.7 months with 68% of patients with an observed DoR \ge6 months and 40% of patients with an observed DoR \ge12 months.
L1223: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L1224: The observed ORR was generally consistent across most patient subgroups, defined by relevant demographic and disease-related characteristics, including across subgroups of patients with platinum-sensitive and platinum-refractory disease and across subgroups with various DLL3 expression cut-offs. Of the 99 patients who received tarlatamab at the proposed dosing regimen, 69 patients had available data regarding platinum sensitivity status. In this subgroup of patients, the ORR was 52% (95% CI: 32%, 71%) in 27 patients with platinum-resistant SCLC (defined as &lt; 90 days after end of first line platinum therapy to date of progression) and 31% (95% CI: 18%, 47%) in 42 patients with platinum-sensitive SCLC (defined as ≥90 days after end of first line platinum therapy to date of progression). Although the ORR point estimates differ between these subgroups, the 95% CI overlap, and FDA considers that a true difference in efficacy based on platinum sensitivity status is unlikely with tarlatamab.
L1225: For this application, the proposed indication, SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy, differs from the population enrolled in Study 20200491, SCLC with disease progression after receiving previous treatment with platinum-based chemotherapy and at least one other line of prior therapy. Overall, the ORR and DoR data from the primary efficacy population in Study 20200491, which enrolled a more heavily treated patient population (3rd line or greater), is greater than the ORR and DoR observed with topotecan, the only available therapy with traditional approval from the FDA for patients with SCLC previously treated with first-line chemotherapy (ORR of 24% in platinum sensitive disease and 2-7% in platinum resistant disease). Review of prior therapies in the primary efficacy population from Study 20200491 showed that 52% of patients received a topoisomerase I inhibitor as second-line therapy. Of the remaining patients who did not receive a prior topoisomerase I inhibitor, the grand majority (89%) received other second line therapies considered treatment options per U.S. standard of care. The ORR in the subgroups of patients who received prior topoisomerase I inhibitor; and who did not receive prior topoisomerase I inhibitor (with or without patients who received standard of care therapies), were generally consistent across subgroups and consistent with the ORR observed in the overall study population. Furthermore, it is not expected that treatment with additional therapies following platinum-based chemotherapy would increase the likelihood of response to tarlatamab relative to treatment with tarlatamab used as second-line therapy following platinum-based chemotherapy. In light of the observed results and particularly considering the ORR observed with tarlatamab in patients with platinum-resistant SCLC, which far exceeds ORR reported with standard of care therapies, the available data for tarlatamab supports the proposed indication for treatment of SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L1226: Data from Part 3 of Study 20200491 and from patients who received the tarlatamab 10 mg dose in Study 20160323 were considered supportive of the primary efficacy results from Study 20200491.
L1227: These results provide substantial evidence of effectiveness of tarlatamab for the treatment of adult patients with extensive-stage small cell lung cancer (ES-SCLC) with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.

L1245: ## The FDA's Assessment:

In Part 3 of the protocol (n=34), patients were hospitalized for 24 hours post tarlatamab infusion for cycle 1 day 1 and day 8 doses. In supportive Study 20160323 (n=54) patients were hospitalized for 48 hours post tarlatamab infusion on Cycle 1 Day 1
L1247: and Cycle 1 Day 8 doses.

FDA agrees that the safety data provided allows for an appropriate analysis of adverse events and the characterization of the safety profile of tarlatamab.

L1252: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1253: The primary analysis of safety for the proposed indication is based on the integrated safety analysis of pooled data from subjects with SCLC who were treated with tarlatamab monotherapy at a dose of 10 or 100 mg Q2W in pivotal phase 2 Study 20200491 and FIH phase 1 Study 20160323; this population is the primary focus in this section. Supportive integrated safety data across all tarlatamab monotherapy doses (Studies 20200491, 20160323, and 20200040) and all tarlatamab combination therapy doses (with pembrolizumab in Part C of Study 20160323, AMG 404 in Study 20200439, and atezolizumab or durvalumab \pm carboplatin and etoposide in Study 20200469) are provided in Module 2.7.4, Summary of Clinical Safety (SCS) and in the Integrated Summary of Safety (ISS).
L1254: Demographics, baseline disease characteristics, exposure, adverse events, and other safety assessments were summarized using the safety analysis set, which included all subjects enrolled who received \ge 1 dose of tarlatamab in monotherapy cohorts and \ge 1 dose of any investigational product in combination cohorts.
L1255: Throughout the tarlatamab clinical development program, safety was continuously evaluated by Amgen through routine signal detection activities such as review of all serious adverse events across all the clinical studies.
L1256: Four adverse events of interest (EOIs) were analyzed for tarlatamab: cytokine release syndrome (CRS), immune effector cell-associated neurotoxicity syndrome (ICANS) and associated neurological events, neurological events (nervous system disorders [system organ class]/psychiatric disorders [system organ class]), and neutropenia. These events were clinically monitored through protocol designated reporting of clinical signs and symptoms, as well as scheduled assessments of laboratory tests.

L1296: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1297: FDA's safety population included patients from Study 20200491 and Study 20160323 (n=187) with extensive stage SCLC who were treated with tarlatamab monotherapy at the recommended dose of 10 mg. Median age was 66 years (range 35 to 82); other demographic information: male (65%), White (70%), Asian, (26%), Hispanic/Latino (2.1%) and Black (2.1%).
L1298: Although the safety patient population included a lower proportion of females compared to the US population with SCLC, it is considered generally representative of the overall population of patients with advanced SCLC in the US, except with regards to race/ethnicity. Despite these limitations, the overall data is considered supportive of the proposed indication in the US population. However, a postmarketing commitment (PMC) will be issued for analyses from ongoing or planned clinical trials enrolling a sufficient representation of U.S. racial and ethnic minority patients that is reflective of the US population of patients with SCLC to further characterize the safety and efficacy of tarlatamab in these patients.

L1342: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1343: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1344: ## **Data:**
L1345: The study centers were monitored by contract research organizations for Study 20200491 and by Amgen staff and partners in Study 20160323. Monitors were responsible for reviewing adherence to the protocol; compliance with GCP; and the completeness, accuracy, and consistency of the data. Direct access to subject medical and laboratory records was permitted to verify entries on the study-specific eCRFs.
L1346: Investigator staff training was provided by Amgen Development during investigator meetings, initiation, and routine monitoring visits. The sponsor organized investigator and clinical research associate meetings before study start and during the study to provide information on the investigational product, the study rationale and design, responsibilities under ICH GCP, and training on the detailed study requirements.
L1347: Central laboratories were used in the studies. Where local laboratories were used, their participation in internal and external quality control, quality assurance, and accreditation schemes was evaluated by the study monitors.
L1348: The investigators were responsible for all data entered in the eCRFs and documented their review and approval of the data by signing a form verifying the validity and completeness of the data. The investigator was responsible for appropriate retention of essential study documents.
L1349: Case report form data were captured (via data entry by study center personnel in an Amgen database system [Amgen RAVE Electronic Data Capture]). Data quality checks were applied using manual and electronic verification methods. An audit trail to support data query resolution and any modification to the data was maintained.
L1350: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1351: No issues were identified regarding data integrity or submission quality that had an effect on the safety review.
L1352: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1353: FDA agrees that the information in the application, including safety datasets and individual case narratives, was adequate to support the safety analysis. There were findings noted from the GCP site inspections as described in Section 4.1. The Applicant conducted a 100% SDR/SDV for all clinical sites that participated in Study 20200491 which yielded updated data for efficacy and safety that did not impact FDA's interpretation of the trial results in a meaningful way. The updated 90-day safety datasets with DCO of October 2, 2023, were used for FDA's safety review and labeling.
L1354: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1355: ## Data:
L1356: Not applicable, please see Applicant's position.
L1357: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1358: Standard methodologies were used to categorize adverse events. The analyses used Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) version 26.0 for initial ISS analysis and MedDRA version 26.1 for 90-day update. The severity of each adverse event was graded using National Cancer Institute – Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) version 5.0 for Studies 20200491 and 20200469. The severity of each adverse event for Studies 20160323, (b) (4) was converted from CTCAE version 4.0 to 5.0, if applicable. A description of the methodology used for this conversion is provided in the Clinical Events Classification Charter (ISS Module 5.3.5.3). Events of interest were evaluated based on standardized MedDRA queries.
L1359: The collection of adverse events in the clinical studies was also appropriate. The protocols defined adverse events and serious adverse events, as well as the reporting procedures. Adverse events were collected after enrollment through the safety follow-up visit. All adverse events considered to be related to investigational product and all serious adverse events regardless of relationship were required to be followed until stabilization or reversibility. Treatment-emergent adverse events were defined as any adverse event with an onset date between the date of first dose and up to and including 42 days after the date of last dose of investigational product or the end of study date, whichever was earlier.
L1360: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1361: FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of AE categorization. The FDA review was completed using MedRA preferred terms (PTs) and CTCAE grade as well as custom grouped terms (GT) when performing independent analyses of AEs.
L1362: The applicant upgraded all adverse events (AEs) excluding cytokine release syndrome (CRS) and laboratory data, from CTCAE version 4.0 to CTCAE version 5.0 if all the necessary data was available. CRS AEs graded using Lee, 2014 criteria were also upgraded to ASTCT 2019 grading scale (Lee, 2019) if all the data was available but was limited to the availability of temperature and vasopressor use as data regarding hypoxia/oxygen intervention were not collected and/or consistently reported.
L1363: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1364: ## Data:
L1365: In Studies 20200491 and 20160323, all protocol-required laboratory assessments, including routine hematology and serum chemistry panels, were to be conducted in accordance with the laboratory manual and the schedule of assessments in the protocols. Laboratory values were graded using the current version of NCI CTCAE and converted if necessary (see above). Vital signs, physical measurements, and electrocardiograms (ECGs) were also assessed during study visits as outlined in the schedule of assessments. Clinically significant abnormal vital signs or findings were to be reported as adverse events.
L1366: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1367: The clinical monitoring of subject safety was considered adequate for the expected toxicities associated with tarlatamab treatment. The time points and analyses of clinical tests were defined in the study protocols.
L1368: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1369: FDA agrees that the clinical monitoring of patient safety was adequate. For Studies 20200491 and 20160323, multiple safety assessments were conducted on the days of screening and infusion which included physical examinations, neurological examinations, vital signs, height and weight, performance status according to Karnofsky and laboratory evaluation (hematology, chemistry panels, urinalysis, and pregnancy tests).
L1370: For Study 20200491 during Cycle 1 (Parts 1 and 2) vitals were performed every 15 minutes during infusion and during the first 2 hours after end of infusion (EOI), followed by every 30 minutes from 2 to 4 hours after EOI, every hour from 4 to 8 hours after EOI, 12 hours post-EOI, 20 hours post-EOI, 24 hours post-EOI. After 24 hours post-EOI, vital signs should be assessed per institutional standards. For Cycle 1 (Part 3) vitals were assessed every 30 minutes during infusion, during first 4 hours post-EOI, every hour from 4-8 hours EOI, 12 hours post-EOI, 20 hours post-EOI and 24 hours post-EOI.
L1371: ECGs were collected pre and post infusion as part of standard monitoring for QT prolongation. Triplicate ECGs were collected pre-infusion on Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1) and post-infusion on C2D1
L1372: and C2D15 for the first approximately 50 patients in Part 1. ECGs were collected pre and post infusion for those patients who did not have triplicated ECGs performed.
L1373: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1374: ## Deaths
L1375: ## Data:
L1376: ## *Monotherapy Studies*
L1377: Overall, fatal adverse events were reported for 19 subjects (4.2%) treated with tarlatamab monotherapy; the most frequently reported (&gt; 1 subject) fatal adverse events were aspiration, cardio-respiratory arrest, neuroendocrine carcinoma of the prostate, pneumonia, and respiratory failure (2 subjects [0.4%] each (Table 44). The 2 fatal events of respiratory failure were considered treatment related (ISS Table 14-6.2.15). Both subjects had significant confounders.
L1378: In the 10 mg dose group, fatal adverse events were reported for 5 subjects (3.1%) with no preferred term reported for more than 1 subject. In the 100 mg group, fatal adverse events were reported for 6 subjects (3.7%); all events occurred in 1 subject except cardio-respiratory arrest (2 subjects [1.2%]).
L1379: ## *Combination Therapy Studies*
L1380: No fatal adverse events were reported for subjects treated with tarlatamab combination therapy in Studies 20160323 Part C(b) (4)

L1408: Review of fatal adverse events across the tarlatamab clinical development program as of the data cutoff dates did not identify any meaningful trends suggesting a causal association between tarlatamab use and fatalities.
L1409: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1410: FDA confirmed the information above and reviewed all narratives for on-treatment deaths in the pooled safety population. There were 22 fatal events which were attributed to disease progression. Narratives were provided for 10 patients who had fatal adverse events not due to disease progression who were treated with tarlatamab at the recommended dose of 10 mg; 5 of these events occurred more than 30 days after discontinuing study therapy (all n=1, pneumonia, necrotizing pneumonia, dyspnea, hepatic failure and myocardial infarction).
L1411: The narratives for the five patients with on-treatment deaths not due to disease progression that were within 30 days of discontinuation of study treatment are reviewed in further detail below.

L1438: ## **Safety Update as of 02 October 2023**
L1439: The incidence of serious adverse events in the monotherapy studies that occurred in ≥ 1% of subjects at the doses of 10 and 100 mg are shown in Table 49.
L1440: Overall, as of 02 October 2023, serious adverse events were reported for 289 subjects (59.7%) treated with tarlatamab monotherapy. Consistent with the initial ISS analysis, the most frequently reported (\ge 3\% of subjects) serious adverse events by preferred term included CRS (26.9%), pneumonia (4.1%), and pyrexia (3.9%).
L1441: For the 10 mg target dose, serious adverse events were reported for 104 subjects (55.6%) as of 02 October 2023. Consistent with the initial ISS analysis, the most frequently reported (\ge 3\% of subjects) serious adverse events by preferred term included CRS (23.5%), pneumonia (3.7%), pyrexia (3.7%); in contrast, hyponatremia was also reported in \ge 3\% of subjects (3.2%).
L1442: For the 100 mg target dose, serious adverse events were reported for 103 subjects (63.2%) as of 02 October 2023. Consistent with the initial ISS analysis, the most frequently reported (\ge 3\% of subjects) serious adverse events by preferred term were CRS (34.4%), ICANS (6.1%), pyrexia (4.9%); hyponatremia (4.3%); pneumonia and encephalopathy (3.7% each); and COVID-19 (3.1%).
L1443: In the combination therapy studies, the subject incidence of serious adverse events ranged from 43.6% to 56.5% across studies, compared to 59.5% treated with tarlatamab monotherapy. The most frequently reported serious adverse event was CRS (90 DSU ISS Table 14-6.2.6).
