12.3 PK	
The proposed labeling is acceptable upon the Applicant and FDA reaching agreements to the FDA recommended revisions to the labeling, which included updates regarding formatting and clear reporting of descriptive statistics.
12.3 Pharmacokinetics
Tarlatamab pharmacokinetic parameters are presented as geometric mean (CV%) unless otherwise specified. The exposure of tarlatamab increased dose proportionally in the evaluated dose range of IMDELLTRA 1 mg to 100 mg every 2 weeks (10 times the highest approved recommended dosage). Tarlatamab steady state exposures were achieved by Cycle 2 Day 15. pharmacokinetic exposures are summarized for the recommended dosage of IMDELLTRA in Table 15.
Distribution
Tarlatamab steady state volume of distribution is 8.6 L (18.3%).
Metabolism
Tarlatamab- is expected to be metabolized into small peptides by catabolic pathways.
Elimination
Tarlatamab's median terminal elimination half-life (min, max) is 11.2 (4.3 to 26.5) days and the estimated systemic clearance is 0.65 L/day (44%) in patients with SCLC.
Specific Populations
No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of tarlatamab
L2046: were observed based on age (32 to 82 years), bodyweight (35 to 149 kg), sex, race (White and Asian), mild or moderate renal impairment (eGFR \ge 30 to < 90 mL/min), or mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal (ULN) and AST > ULN).
The effects of severe renal impairment (eGFR 15 to 29 mL/min), end-stage renal disease (eGFR <15 mL/min), or moderate to severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 1.5 x ULN, any AST) on the pharmacokinetics of tarlatamab are unknown.
Effects of Tarlatamab on CYP450 Substrates
Tarlatamab causes transient release of cytokines that may suppress CYP450 enzymes and may result in an increased exposure of concomitant CYP substrates during and up to 14 days after occurrence of cytokine release syndrome [see Clinical Pharmacology (12.2)].

L2094: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2095: Overall, the Applicant's PPK model is generally acceptable for the purposes of characterizing PK and predicting exposure following intravenous infusion of tarlatamab in patients with advanced SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L2096: The Applicant's parameter values for the final model were generally precisely estimated.
L2097: Residual standard error (RES) for all structural model parameters were low (&lt;10.7%). RSE for covariate effect parameters were also low (&lt;24.8%), except for the effect of ADA on CL (RSE of 51.4%). All variance parameters were also estimated precisely with RSEs lower than 11.4%.
L2098: Shrinkage of the inter-individual random effects was low to moderate (&lt;30%). The predictions and residuals are symmetrically distributed without any significant trends. The VPC plots showed acceptable model predictive performance across different studies with a slight tendency to overpredict tarlatamab concentration at 5th percentile during the elimination phase.
L2099: No clinically relevant differences in exposure according to patient characteristics were identified, including age (32-82 years), bodyweight (35-149 kg), sex, race (White and Asian), ethnicity, albumin, number of prior anti-cancer therapies, ECOG score, tumor burden, ADA
L2100: status, mild (N=156) or moderate (N=37) renal impairment (eGFR \ge 30 to &lt; 90 mL/min), or mild (N=111) hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal (ULN) and AST &gt; ULN).
L2101: ## Model Derived Tarlatamab PK Parameters
L2102: The model derived PK parameters for tarlatamab in Study 20200491 based on population PK simulations following the proposed dosage regimen (1 mg on day 1, 10 mg on day 8, 15 and q2w thereafter) were summarized in Table 4. Cmax was defined as the concentration at the end of infusion.

L2107: ## PK analyses to support the proposed repriming strategy after dose delay
L2108: As shown in
L2109: and Figure 38, the simulated Ctrough and Cavg levels remained relatively high after a 21 or 28-day dose delay following the administration of full dose of 10mg on day 8 or day 15, respectively. Although the exposure was approximately 60% lower after a 14-day delay post step-up dose administration compared to the treatment plan with no dose delay, the low incidence rate and mild CRS observed in patients following the step-up dose and with dose delay in Study 20200491 (For detailed information, please refer to Table 22 in section 6.3.2.2) indicated that the exposure following a 14-day delay post step-up dose administration is sufficient to mitigate the incidence and severity of CRS following administration of 10 mg tarlatamab. These analysis
L2110: results support the Applicant's proposed repriming strategy after dose delay.

L2119: ## 19.4.4. Exposure-Response Analysis
L2120: ## 19.4.4.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L2121: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2122: The E-R efficacy analysis generally supports the proposed dosage of tarlatamab (10 mg) in patients with advanced SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L2123: The exposure efficacy analysis suggested that higher tarlatamab exposure associated with higher ORR across the dose range of 3 to 100 mg, evaluated in studies 20160323 and 20200491. The ORR appeared to plateau at doses of 10 mg and above. The secondary endpoints (DCR, PFS, OS, BTSR) also demonstrated similar efficacy across the evaluated dose range of 3 to 100 mg. These analyses based on secondary endpoints are considered to be exploratory due to difficulty of interpretation in the absence of an appropriate control arm. Except for the sum of tumor diameter at baseline (BTSR) being identified as a covariate associated with higher hazard for OS, no other covariates were identified to have a clinically meaningful impact on ORR, DCR, PFS, OS and BTSR.
L2124: Overall, there are no clinically significant exposure-response relationships for efficacy over the exposure range observed between 10 mg and 100 mg. This flat ER relationship for efficacy is consistent with similar ORRs observed in 10 mg and 100 mg treatment arm in the pivotal trial.
L2125: ## 19.4.4.2. ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L2126: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2127: Using pooled data across Studies 20160323 and 20200491, the relationship between model-estimated averaged concentration over the first cycle and key efficacy endpoints were explored. These analyses confirmed that the exposures associated with the proposed dosing regimen of 10 mg Q2W maximizes the efficacy of tarlatamab.
L2128: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Characterize the relationships between tarlatamab serum exposures and key efficacy endpoints, including objective response rate (ORR) (complete response [CR] and partial response [PR]), disease control rate (DCR, comprising CR, PR or stable disease [SD]), progression-free survival (PFS), overall survival (OS), duration of
L2129: response (DOR), and best tumor size response (BTSR, based on percentage change in sum of target lesion diameters from baseline)

The consistent results were obtained using the Cavg,event or Cavg,ss exposure metrics for evaluating the exposure efficacy relationships, when compared with the exposure efficacy relationships obtained using Cavg,first cycle.

None of the covariates evaluated in this analysis resulted in clinically meaningful change in efficacy and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.	Overall, the Applicant's ER analyses for efficacy are deemed acceptable.

L2169: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2170: The E-R efficacy analyses supports the proposed dosage of tarlatamab (10 mg) in patients with advanced SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L2171: ## **ORR (Primary endpoint)**
L2172: Graphical analyses for ORR suggested an efficacy benefit for patients with tarlatamab C_{\text{avg, first cycle}} above Q1 (Figure 39 and Figure 40). Exposure efficacy analyses indicated a positive relationship between tarlatamab exposure and ORR, with higher ORR observed at higher exposure levels. The ORR appeared to plateau at dose of 10 mg and above. The model estimated ORR probabilities were 26.2, 31.4 and 33.6% at doses of 3, 10 and 100 mg Q2W, respectively (Figure 42). Additionally, the estimated DCR probabilities were 52.0%, 60.8% and 64.5%, respectively (Figure 42). The corresponding tarlatamab median C_{\text{avg, first cycle}} values were
L2173: 0.2, 0.773 and 6.77 ug/mL at doses of 3, 10 and 100 mg Q2W, respectively. No significant covariate was identified for both ORR and DCR. Additionally, the exposure metrics (C_{\text{avg, first cycle}}) used in this exposure efficacy analysis were derived from the PPK analysis based on the patients' actual tarlatamab dosing records over the first cycle and patient specific covariates identified in the PPK analysis. Considering the first response (PR or better) was assessed approximately 6 weeks after the initial treatment of tarlatamab (Figure 13 in section 8.1.2), the C_{\text{avg during first cycle}} has integrated the treatment modification information including any treatment discontinuation or dose interruption during the first cycle. Therefore, C_{\text{avg, first cycle}} is an appropriate exposure metric for evaluating the relationship between tarlatamab exposure and ORR. Based on the identified E-R relationship, the entire exposure range following administration of 1/10 mg has likely already reached a level of exposure that may lead to a plateau response (Figure 42). This is consistent with the observed data which indicated that dose interruptions did not appear to impact the tarlatamab responses in the 1/10 mg cohort (For the detailed information, please refer to Table 23 in Section 6.3.2.2).
L2174: ## DCR, PFS, OS, DOR and BTSR (secondary endpoints)
L2175: Overall, E-R analyses indicated that, across the evaluated exposure range, the secondary efficacy endpoints (DCR, PFS, OS and BTSR) were significantly associated with tarlatamab C_{\text{avg, first cycle}} and appeared to reach a plateau with exposure at 10 mg Q2W. This is consistent with the results obtained for the primary efficacy endpoint (ORR).
L2176: The univariate CoxPH analyses for time-to-events (PFS and OS) also indicated significant associations between tarlatamab C_{\text{avg, first cycle}} and PFS and OS (Table 71). The E-R trend for DOR is not statistically significant possibly due to the limited sample size (~28 patients in each quartile). The multivariate COX regression analyses identified the sum of tumor diameter at baseline as a covariate that was significantly associated with higher hazard for OS. No other significant covariates were identified for PFS (Table 72). Of note, FDA does not consider these endpoints interpretable in the absence of an appropriate control arm (see Section 8.1.2) and these analyses are considered to be exploratory.
L2177: Please note that the efficacy of tarlatamab was evaluated at two dose levels, 10 and 100 mg, in the dose expansion cohorts in study 20200491. There was no clinically significant E-R relationship for efficacy over the exposure range between 10 and 100 mg.
L2178: ## 19.4.4.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2179: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2180: The E-R safety analysis generally supports the proposed dosage of tarlatamab (10 mg) in patients with advanced SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L2181: The E-R safety analyses indicated that there was an exposure-response relationship between tarlatamab exposure and neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS with a higher risk of any grade neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS at higher exposure.
L2182: Multivariate logistic regression analyses indicated that the higher sum of tumor lesion diameter at baseline and exposure to prior anti-PD1 treatment were associated with higher probability of neurologic toxicity including ICANS, while the ECOG status at baseline was associated with higher probability of neutropenia.
L2183: In addition, the observed proportion of patients experiencing neutropenia and neurologic toxicity including ICANS following a 100 mg target dose, was higher than those observed in 10 mg target dose. This dose-response trend was consistent with the estimated E-R relationship for safety.
L2184: ## 19.4.4.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L2185: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2186: No clear trends for exposure-response relationships were identified for grade \ge 3 adverse events, grade \ge 3 treatment-related adverse events, and grade \ge 3 adverse events of interest of neurological events, ICANS, and CRS. A shallow trend was observed for higher percentage of subjects experiencing grade \ge 3 neutropenia with increasing tarlatamab exposures. No clinically relevant impact on QTc interval was observed at the proposed clinical regimen of 10 mg Q2W.
L2187: The results of the exposure-response analysis suggest that the proposed clinical regimen of 10 mg Q2W reaches the plateau of efficacy with a limited impact on neutropenia and provides a reasonable balance of benefit-risk in this patient population. None of the covariates evaluated in this analysis resulted in clinically meaningful changes in the drug's effect on efficacy and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.

L2210: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2211: The E-R safety analysis generally supports the proposed dosage of tarlatamab (10 mg) in patients with advanced SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy.
L2212: Applicant's exposure safety analyses are considered acceptable. In response to the FDA's IR, the Applicant conducted several additional analyses to further explore safety-related aspects of the treatment with tarlatamab in patients with advanced SCLC with disease progression on or after platinum-based chemotherapy. These analyses showed that there was a positive E-R relationship between tarlatamab exposure and neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS with a higher risk of any grade neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS at higher exposure.
L2213: The E-R analyses for CRS could be potentially confounded by the mitigation approach (step-up dosing regimen) utilized to manage the CRS risk at doses of 3 mg and above. Consequently, the analysis results may not accurately reflect the true relationship between tarlatamab exposure and CRS risk.
L2214: No apparent trend between tarlatamab exposure metrics and the incidence rate of any grade ICANS was identified.
L2215: Additionally, a comparison of overall neurologic toxicity including ICANS between 10 and 100 mg target doses indicated a consistent relationship between tarlatamab dose, exposure and neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS.
L2216: ## CRS
L2217: Based on the data from Study 20200491, the overall CRS incidence rates were 30% for Grade 1, 20% for Grade 2 and 1% for Grade 3 following administration of 1/10 mg tarlatamab, while the CRS incidence rates were slightly higher following administration of 1/100 mg tarlatamab with Grade 1 at 35%, Grade 2 at 24% and Grade 3 at 5%. The analyses conducted by the Applicant showed no clear trends in E-R relationships for Grade \ge 3 CRS. However, these findings are not considered reliable due to the limited number of patients (16/412) who experienced Grade \ge 3 CRS in safety dataset.
L2218: The additional E-R analyses for Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2 CRS suggested a slightly positive trend, with a higher CRS rate at higher exposure levels. This relationship may indicate that 1 mg may
L2219: not adequately stimulate the immune system for a higher target dose of 100 mg when compared to a lower target dose of 10 mg. However, it should be noted the derived E-R relationship for CRS based on the tarlatamab safety dataset may be confounded by the mitigation approach of the 1 mg step-up dose that had been utilized to mitigate the CRS risk at the dose of 3 mg and above and may not accurately reflect the true relationship between tarlatamab exposure and CRS risk.
L2220: To visualize the impact of body weight, age and DDL3 on the CRS risk following administration of 1/10 mg tarlatamab, sankey plots were generated for CRS grade and tocilizumab use by BW quartiles, age quartiles and DDL3 cut point based on the data from Study 20200491 (1/10 mg) in response to the FDA's IR. As shown in Figure 45, Figure 46, and Figure 47, no apparent trend of CRS incidence rate or severity was found by age quartiles, BW quartiles or DDL3 cut points.

L2232: ## Neutropenia
L2233: The Applicant's exposure safety analyses indicated a shallow positive trend, with a higher percentage of patients experiencing Grade \ge 3 neutropenia as tarlatamab exposures during the first treatment cycle increased.
L2234: Considering that the majority of neutropenia events occurred later beyond the first treatment cycle, the E-R relationships for Grade \ge 1, Grade \ge 2 and Grade \ge 3 neutropenia were assessed using the different exposure metrics, C_{\text{avg}}, C_{\text{max}} and C_{\text{trough}}, which were derived according to the following conditions.
L2235: --C_{\text{max}} and C_{\text{trough}} were derived from the last dosing interval before the event time of neutropenia AEs.
L2236: --C_{\text{avg}} was calculated from the last complete dosing interval over 2 weeks before the event time of neutropenia AEs.
L2237: As shown in Figure 48, the incidence rate for Grade \ge 1, Grade \ge 2 and Grade \ge 3 neutropenia were found to be correlated with the exposure to tarlatamab, with a higher incidence rate of neutropenia observed at higher levels of tarlatamab exposure. It appears that C_{\text{trough}} was the exposure metric most strongly associated with the likelihood of neutropenia events, as evident from the lowest Akaike information criterion (AIC) value compared to C_{\text{avg}} and C_{\text{max}}.
L2238: Multivariate logistic regression analyses indicated that ECOG status at baseline was associated with higher probability of neutropenia. Figure 49 showed the model predicted mean probability and observed Grade \ge 1, Grade \ge 2 and Grade \ge 3 neutropenia events with increasing tarlatamab exposure.

L2256: ## Neurologic toxicity including ICANS
L2257: In response to the FDA's IR, the Applicant conducted the exposure safety analysis for neurologic toxicity including ICANS (Grade\ge 1, Grade\ge 2 and Grade\ge 3) using tarlatamab exposure metrics, C_{\text{avg,ss}} and C_{\text{max,ss}}, which were derived according to the following conditions.
L2258: - -- C_{\text{avg,ss}}: average concentration over the dosing interval based on nominal dosing on Cycle 2 Day 1
- -- C_{\text{max,ss}}: maximum concentration over the dosing interval based on nominal dosing on Cycle 2
L2259: ## Day 1.
L2260: As shown in Figure 51, a positive exposure response relationship was observed for patients experienced Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2 neurologic toxicity including ICANS. No trend was found for patients experiencing Grade \ge 3 neurologic toxicity including ICANS. Consistent with the findings from graphical analysis, a statistically significant relationship was identified between tarlatamab exposure and Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2 neurologic toxicity including ICANS. Higher tarlatamab exposure was associated with a greater incidence rate of Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2 neurologic toxicity including ICANS. No statistically significant positive exposure response relationship for Grade \ge 3 neurologic toxicity including ICANS was identified, likely due to the limited number of patients (41/412) who experienced Grade \ge 3 neurologic toxicity including ICANS in the safety dataset.
L2261: Multivariate logistic regression analyses indicated that the higher sum of tumor lesion diameter at baseline and exposure to prior anti-PD1 treatment were associated with higher probability of neurologic toxicity including ICANS.
L2262: In addition, it was observed that a higher proportion of patients experienced Grade \ge 1 (78%) and grade \ge 2 (46%) neurologic toxicity including ICANS following a 100 mg target dose, relative to 10 mg target dose (69% for Grade \ge 1 and 31% for Grade \ge 2 events). This trend was consistent with the estimated E-R relationship when C_{\text{avg,ss}} was employed as a predictor variable. The predicted mean probability of experiencing Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2 neurologic toxicity including ICANS at median C_{\text{avg,ss}} corresponding to either 10 mg or 100 mg dose levels is comparable to the observed data (Table 75).

L2275: ## 19.4.4.5. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L2276: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2277: No clear trends for exposure-response relationships were identified for grade \ge 3 adverse events, grade \ge 3 treatment-related adverse events, and grade \ge 3 adverse events of interest of neurological events, ICANS, and CRS. A shallow trend was observed for higher percentage of
L2278: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761344}
## {Tarlatamab}
L2279: subjects experiencing grade \ge 3 neutropenia with increasing tarlatamab exposures. No clinically relevant impact on QTc interval was observed at the proposed clinical regimen of 10 mg Q2W.
L2280: The results of the exposure-response analysis suggest that the proposed clinical regimen of 10 mg Q2W reaches the plateau of efficacy with a limited impact on neutropenia and provides a reasonable balance of benefit-risk in this patient population. None of the covariates evaluated in this analysis resulted in clinically meaningful changes in the drug's effect on efficacy and therefore do not warrant a dose adjustment.
L2281: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2282: FDA agrees based on results from study 20200491 and study 2016323, tarlatamab represents a therapeutic approach intended for addressing unmet medical needs in patient with SCLC, a life-threatening condition.
L2283: FDA agrees that the proposed dose regimen of 1 mg on cycle 1 day 1, followed by 10 mg on cycle 1 day 8, 15 and every 2 weeks did not raise any significant safety concerns. CRS risk was effectively mitigated by a single 1 mg step-up dose and retreatment plans after dose delay. The E-R efficacy and safety relationships generally support the proposed dosage regimen.
L2284: However, a positive E-R relationship was identified between tarlatamab exposure and neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS with a higher risk of any grade neutropenia or neurologic toxicity including ICANS at higher exposure. Therefore, the appropriate risk management strategies may be necessary to ensure a favorable risk/benefit balance.
L2285: A positive E-R relationship between tarlatamab exposure and efficacy endpoints was observed across the dose levels evaluated (3-100 mg). The efficacy appears to reach plateau at doses of 10 mg and above, supporting the proposed target doses.
L2286: Furthermore, FDA agrees that no clinically relevant impact on QTc interval was observed. Although Applicant's analysis determines a statistically significant positive C-QTc relationship, this relationship is very shallow, not clinically significant, and unlikely to be due to interaction with hERG channel since tarlatamab is a large therapeutic protein.

L2291: Selectivity & matrix effect	A total of 10 individual lots (healthy: 2 males, 2 females; 4 SCLC patients, 1 hemolytic and 1 lipemic) of human serum samples were tested unspiked and spiked at the LLOQ (0.0203 ng/mL), LQC (0.0650 ng/mL) and ULOQ (133 ng/mL) levels. L2311: Figure 2 displays two graphs, A and B, illustrating the Applicant's data on Tarlatamab-induced release of human cytokines.
Graph A (HCT-116): Shows cytokine levels (pg/mL) versus AMG 757 concentration (pM) when HCT-116 cells are co-cultured with human PBMC donor. Cytokine levels (IL-2, IL-4, IL-6, IL-10, TNF, IFN-\gamma) remain very low across the tested concentration range (0 to 10^4 pM).
Graph B (SHP-77): Shows cytokine levels (pg/mL) versus AMG 757 concentration (pM) when SHP-77 cells are co-cultured with human PBMC donor. Cytokine levels (IL-2, IL-4, IL-6, IL-10, TNF, IFN-\gamma) show a dose-dependent increase, particularly for IL-2, IL-4, and IL-6, reaching levels above 1000 pg/mL at 10^4 pM.

L2322: DeLLphi-301 Study Design
Phase 2, open-label study (NCT05060016)
Key Inclusion Criteria
ES-SCLC
Previous treatment with \ge 2 lines (including platinum-doublet)
ECOG PS 0-1
Measurable disease
Treated and stable brain metastases allowed
Part 1: Dose Evaluation
Subjects are randomized (R 1:1) to:
Tarlatamab 10 mg (n = 88)
1 mg on Day 1, followed by 10 mg on Days 8, 15, and Q2W thereafter
Tarlatamab 100 mg (n = 88)
1 mg on Day 1, followed by 10 mg on Days 8, 15, and Q2W thereafter
The results lead to Dose Selection*.
Part 2: Dose Expansion
Tarlatamab 10 mg (n = 12)
Same dosing as in Part 1
Part 3: Reduced Inpatient Monitoring Period
Tarlatamab 10 mg (n = 34)
Same dosing as in Part 1
Primary Endpoint: ORR per RECIST v1.1 by BICR, TEAEs, tarlatamab serum concentrations
Secondary Endpoints Included: DOR, DCR, PFS per RECIST v1.1 by BICR, OS

L2328: Tarlatamab - 100 mg (N = 71)
Waterfall plot showing the Best % Change From Baseline in Sum of Diameters for 71 subjects treated with Tarlatamab 100 mg. The Y-axis ranges from -120 to 160. The plot displays the distribution of responses, categorized by RECIST criteria:

SD (Stable Disease): Cyan bars.

L2333: Flowchart illustrating the Applicant's Schema of Study 20160323.
The study is divided into three main phases: Screening (up to 21 days), Enrollment, and Treatment Period.
Screening (up to 21 days) leads to Enrollment.
Enrollment leads to Treatment Period.
Treatment Period includes the following parts:
Part A1f: Monotherapy: tarlatamab dose exploration R/R SCLC (N~120). This part leads to MTD/RP2D determination.
Part Cc: tarlatamab + pembrolizumabb dose exploration R/R SCLC (N~30). This part is linked to Part A1 by the text: "Upon completion of given cohort in Part A1, proceed to first cohort in Part Cc".
Part Dc: tarlatamab additional CRS mitigation strategies (N~30). This part is linked to Part A1 by a dotted line.
The MTD/RP2D determination leads to the following parts:
Part A2: tarlatamab dose expansion R/R SCLC (N~85).
Part A3 (China only): tarlatamab dose confirmation R/R SCLC (N~20).
Part B: tarlatamab dose exploration (N~0) ES SCLC with benefit after platinum regimenc.
Part Ef: tarlatamab 24-hour monitoring R/R SCLC (N~15).
Part F: tarlatamab 8-hour outpatient cohort R/R SCLC (N~30). This part leads to the Wearable Digital Device Substudy (Part F [N~10], US sites only)d.
Part Gf: tarlatamab alternative dosing schedule (day 1, day 8 cohort [N~12] and Q3W cohort [N~20]). This part leads to the Wearable Digital Device Substudy (Part G [N~10], US sites only)e.
The study concludes with End of Treatment, followed by Safety Follow-up and Long-term Follow-up.

L2365: Figure 51 displays four panels showing the relationship between Tarlatamab exposure metrics (C_{\text{avg},\text{ss}} and C_{\text{max},\text{ss}}) and the observed probability of overall neurologic toxicity, including ICANS events (Grade \ge 1 and Grade \ge 2). The figure is structured in two rows (Upper and Lower panels) and two columns (Exposure of Event and Exposure of Non-event).
The Upper panels plot the Probability of Grade 1+ Overall Neurologic Toxicity (Y-axis) against C_{\text{avg},\text{ss}} (X-axis). The Lower panels plot the Probability of Grade 2+ Overall Neurologic Toxicity (Y-axis) against C_{\text{max},\text{ss}} (X-axis).
In all panels, the blue line represents the mean probability, and the shaded area represents the 95% prediction interval of the model-simulated probability. Vertical error bars and open circles represent the 95% CI and mean probability of observed events, respectively. Short vertical lines in the upper and lower panels represent individuals' C_{\text{avg},\text{ss}} (upper panel) or C_{\text{max},\text{ss}} (lower panel).
Specific data points and p-values are labeled on the plots:
Upper Left (Grade 1+ vs C_{\text{avg},\text{ss}}): p-value = 0.0020. Points include QC 87N (78/100), QC 64B (80/100), and QA 201 (80/100).
Upper Right (Grade 1+ vs C_{\text{avg},\text{ss}}): p-value = 0.0004. Points include QC 83B (80/100), QC 83C (80/100), QC 84B (80/100), and QA 70A (80/100).
Lower Left (Grade 2+ vs C_{\text{max},\text{ss}}): p-value = 0.0005. Points include QC 67B (75/100), QC 68B (80/100), and QC 67C (75/100).
Lower Right (Grade 2+ vs C_{\text{max},\text{ss}}): p-value = 0.0000. Points include QC 61B (80/100), QC 61A (80/100), QC 61C (80/100), and QA 54B (80/100).
